At the moment when, concerning the blessings of Jacob, I undertake to discourse (1), I myself ask for the blessing (2) which comes to us from the Lord (3). Who, in effect, among men is capable of narrating (4) what has been said in a spiritual sense, if the heavenly Wisdom does not collaborate with him while he speaks? And (5) who can interpret what has been said by the blessed prophets by means of parables, in veiled words, if the Word himself does not make himself his own interpreter? (This Word) who, in the ancient days first, in Israel, like a lamp, in the time of the Law, was hidden under a bushel in order to illuminate those who were, in the time of the Law, justified by means of circumcision; then (Word who), in the time in which we are, in a language that says everything, appears on the wood, like a seven-branched lamp on a candlestick, in order to call to salvation, to his own light, even to the distant nations (6).

Γασαπο Παρέστω τοίνυν ἡμῖν αὐτὸς ὁ λόγος ἑρμηνεὺς τῶν ἑαυτοῦ μυστηρίων γινόμενος, ἵνα καὶ τὰ τοῦ Ἰωσὴφ ὁράματα τοῖς πιστεύουσιν φανερῶσαι δυνηθῇ. οὐδὲ γὰρ μάτην τοῦτον ὁραματιστὴν ἐκάλουν οἱ ἀδελφοί, εἰ μὴ δυνάμει προεώρων τὸν ἐν αὐτῷ πολιτευό- ΡΟ ρ. μενον λόγον. « εἶπον γὰρ πρὸς ἑαυτοὺς λέγοντες. ἰδοὺ ὁ ὁραματιστὴς ἔρχεται" δεῦτε καὶ ἀποχτείνωμεν αὐτόν, καὶ ἴδωμεν τί ἔσται τὰ ὁράματα αὐτοῦ ». τίνα δὲ ἦν τὰ τούτου ὁράματα, υἱοὶ Ιακώδ, εἴπατε. ἐθεώρει, φησίν, δράγματα ἐν πεδίῳ δεσμούμενα
(7) May he therefore assist us himself, the Word, making himself the interpreter of his own mysteries, so that them too, the visions of Joseph, he may put his power into making clear to the believers. And, in effect, it is not without reason that his brothers called him a visionary, since they did not see in advance the Word, by power, making his dwelling in him. They said in effect, speaking to themselves: "Here comes the visionary! Come, let us kill him, and let us see what will become of his visions!" But what were his visions, sons of Jacob, tell me? -
καὶ τὸ ἑαυτοῦ δράγμα ὀρθούμενον, τὰ δὲ ἡμέτερα πεσόντα᾽ ἐπιστραφέντα δὲ προσεκύνουν τὸ τοῦ Ἰωσὴφ δράγμα. τί ἐν τούτῳ ἠδίκησεν ᾿Ιωσήφ, εἰ τὸ μέλλον ἔσεσθαι ἀπήγγειλεν ὑμῖν; τί παραζηλοῦτε καὶ μισεῖτε τὸν δίκαιον, εἰ θεὸς τούτῳ τὰ ἑαυτοῦ μυστήρια ἀπεκάλυψεν καὶ τὰ ἐπ’ ἐσχάτων γινόμενα δι᾽ ὁραμάτων ἐφανέρωσεν; τί ἐλυπήθητε ἰδόντες χιτῶνα ποικίλον ἐπ᾽ αὐτῷ, εἰ ὁ δίκαιος πατὴρ τοῦτον παρὰ πάντας ἀγαπήσας ἐτίμησεν καὶ ποιμένα ποιμένων εἰς ἐπίσκεψιν ὑμῖν ἀπέστειλεν καὶ μάρτυρα πιστὸν ἐν κόσμῳ προήγαγεν καὶ δράγμα γήρως ὡς ἀπαρχὴν ἅγιον πρωτότοχον ἐκ νεχρῶν ἀνέστησεν; τί ἀγαναχτεῖτε, εἰ ἥλιος καὶ σελήνη καὶ ἕνδεκα ἀστέρες προσεκύνουν αὐτόν, οἱ πάλαι προτυπούμενοι; οὐδὲ Ἰακὼδ καὶ ῬῬαχὴλ ἥλιος καὶ σελήνη ἐχρημάτιζεν, ἀλλ᾽ οὐδὲ γὰρ οὕτω γεγένηται. ἡ μὲν γὰρ Ῥαχὴλ ἤδη τεθνεῶσα ἐν τῷ ἱπποδρόμῳ ἐτέθαπτο, ὁ δὲ ᾿Ιωσὴφ συναντήσας τῷ πατρὶ αὑτοῦ, πεσὼν προσεκύνησεν, ἵνα ὁ τύπος τηρηθῇ καὶ ἡ Ὑ α.,22 ὥνϑϑ ον οδοῦο Ὀδῦρῳο ὕωφρο οὥσδηςο οὐδοῦ τοῦ ὥοξος» δῶν ο). ϑῦθ ϑονωβοῶθ οἠϑῦρ. τοῦ δβοφρο 9αργοςν δῶσ ὥσϑ)ο). ὥσοΐος» 8ῶ.»- ἄφροου “οϑοογὰδ δὕδυ ϑοὺ ςουϑοθε)φρο «οδοῦδ ὕωφρνο οὐὐδρῦ 8ϑοογοῶῦ δορϑονθχο ὅίρειι εἶν γρειμμειιεεζείνιωι β.. ἀμ τς εθὲν θωΐμεμ ἱκι Πρμιρίνι μυρινημείγι ἐπα μπεωβῖν
"He contemplated, he says, in a plain, bound sheaves; and his own sheaf stood upright, but ours had fallen; then having turned toward it, they worshipped the sheaf of Joseph." What rights, in the occurrence, has Joseph violated, if he announced to you there what was to be? Why do you envy and hate the righteous one, if God has revealed to him his own mysteries, and if he has made clear by visions the events of the end of times? Why your saddened faces at the sight of a tunic embroidered on his back, if the Righteous Father (8), in his love preferring him to all, has honored him, and has sent you to visit a Shepherd of shepherds, and has produced in the world a faithful witness and, sheaf of old age (9), like first fruits, has made a holy First-Born rise from among the dead? Why be indignant if the sun and the moon and eleven stars worshipped him? They are there, from ancient times, as prefigurers (10). And neither was Jacob called (11) "Sun," nor Rachel, "Moon." For also the things did not happen that way. Rachel, she, in effect, already dead, lay buried in the hippodrome. As for Joseph, when he had gone to meet his father, having prostrated himself, he worshipped so that the value of the type might be well kept and that the Scripture might appear well saying the truth (12). Where then is accomplished what has been

Ὁ οδηο ϑω- οϑο-ϑοςῦθον τοὺ φοῦθδο ὅδδο οὐ ὅϑοῦδο ϑοδῦο ςοῦ ογομαησδοῦδθόϑοουο»
said, namely: "Shall we then have to come, I and your mother and your brothers, to worship you face to the ground?" if not when (13) the blessed Apostles, together with Joseph and Mary, once arrived on the Mount of Olives, worshipped Christ? Judas was not with them (14). And Joseph, in effect, was not Christ in person, to the point that one would go to believe that this (prophetic vision) was realized in Egypt, but he was the figure of the (Christ) to come, represented by Joseph (15). (16) May he come then, the true and heavenly Joseph, as interpreter, so that, what, through his intermediary, was announced by the blessed Prophets, He himself, again, by interpreting it, might make himself seen! (17) And, in effect, the Scripture has not (thereupon) kept silence in the story of Joseph, but it has well made us see it (18), Him (i.e., the heavenly Joseph), as interpreter. He himself in effect was the interpreter of the hidden mysteries of the Father. And, in effect, the Scripture says: "As for them, they did not perceive (19) that Joseph understood them, for the interpreter was between them as intermediary."

said, namely: 'Must we then come, I and your mother and your brothers, to worship you with our faces to the ground?' If not when (13) the blessed Apostles, together with Joseph and Mary, once arrived on the Mount of Olives, worshipped Christ? Judas was not with them (14). And Joseph, indeed, was not Christ in person, to the point that one would go and believe that this (prophetic vision) was realized in Egypt, but he was the figure of the (Christ) to come, represented by Joseph (15). (16) Let the true and heavenly Joseph come, then, as interpreter, so that what, through his intermediary, was announced by the blessed Prophets, He Himself, once again, by interpreting it, may make Himself seen! (17) And, indeed, the Scripture did not (on this matter) keep silent in the story of Joseph, but it made us see Him well (18), Him (i.e., the heavenly Joseph), as interpreter. He Himself indeed was the interpreter of the hidden mysteries of the Father. And, indeed, the Scripture says: 'As for them, they did not perceive (19) that Joseph understood them, for the interpreter was an intermediary between them.'

soiled the tunic, the embroidered tunic! with the blood of this kid (22), lying and misinterpreting the events of which you were, yourselves, the authors (23)? The father, for his part, agreed with you in recognizing that it was indeed the son's tunic and bore (with pain) the event (24). And you, Judah, you sell Joseph, conquered as you are by 'the concupiscence of the world.' And the foreign Ishmaelites buy him so that he may be led into Egypt and that there he may be put in irons and that, of visions, the hidden meaning may be, by him, unveiled and that he may be, by Pharaoh, summoned and that he may be instituted King of Egypt and sit, in testimony of trust, on the second throne (25); and that wheat, in abundance, may be by him gathered and kept in store; and that the week of years may be accomplished; and that the starving people may be fed and that he may be, by his brothers, worshipped, and recognized by them as father (26). What did the brothers gain by having then killed a kid and deceived their father (20)? For behold, Joseph, in Egypt, received the title (21) of king. Why have you

soiled the tunic, the embroidered tunic! with the blood of this kid (22), lying and misinterpreting the events of which you were, yourselves, the authors (23)? The father, for his part, agreed with you in recognizing that it was indeed the son's tunic and bore (with pain) the event (24). And you, Judah, you sell Joseph, conquered as you are by 'the concupiscence of the world.' And the foreign Ishmaelites buy him so that he may be led into Egypt and that there he may be put in irons and that, of visions, the hidden meaning may be, by him, unveiled and that he may be, by Pharaoh, summoned and that he may be instituted King of Egypt and sit, in testimony of trust, on the second throne (25); and that wheat, in abundance, may be by him gathered and kept in store; and that the week of years may be accomplished; and that the starving people may be fed and that he may be, by his brothers, worshipped, and recognized by them as father (26).

ΡΟ ρ. ὀσβωσδδὸ οσϑοςο Ο5ϑ50). δῦ δοϑωρϑοργὸῦ ὀο Θονῦδ8οογο ογγδ δοδϑοῶ- Θοδοῦο. διφοὈ-ϑωςοδοδοὄοον δογδοδοῦδαο Ὅρφου-ϑρ5-ϑοῦοϑδς)ς» Οδωονὸ
(28), our attention once well fixed, let us see if the blessings pronounced by Jacob truly went, as blessings, upon the sons of Israel. For we find the opposite, namely: on one hand, blessings, which there (in Egypt or in the Bible) are indeed such by formulation; on the other hand, proof of their (the sons of Israel's) guilt, and, presented in their persons, a very ample prophecy which was realized in the lineage of those who sprang from their stock. So much so that the blessed Jacob was speaking, it is true, to his own sons, as if it were a matter of the present, but that what he was saying happened in the last times. And, on one hand, beforehand, he distinguished the blessings by means of which these things (to come) were prophesied; on the other hand, he gave them to the sons of Israel, while proving their transgressions, and he threatened them, withdrawing from such actions (29). How then shall I not glorify the Heavenly King, He who, by using prophets, staged (27) His own mysteries? Furthermore, since an occasion has also arisen to turn to the interpretation of the Blessings

(28), our attention once well fixed, let us see if the blessings pronounced by Jacob truly went, as blessings, upon the sons of Israel. For we find the opposite, namely: on one hand, blessings, which there (in Egypt or in the Bible) are indeed such by formulation; on the other hand, proof of their (the sons of Israel's) guilt, and, presented in their persons, a very ample prophecy which was realized in the lineage of those who sprang from their stock. So much so that the blessed Jacob was speaking, it is true, to his own sons, as if it were a matter of the present, but that what he was saying happened in the last times. And, on one hand, beforehand, he distinguished the blessings by means of which these things (to come) were prophesied; on the other hand, he gave them to the sons of Israel, while proving their transgressions, and he threatened them, withdrawing from such actions (29).

Ἑὑρίσζομεν δὲ καὶ τὸν μακάριον ᾿Ισαὰκ τὰ ὅμοια πεποιηκότα, καὶ τὸν μὲν Ἰακὼδ τὸν νεώτερον υἱὸν εὐλογοῦντα, καὶ πάντα τούτῳ χαριζόμενον διὰ τὸν ἐξ αὐτοῦ μέλλοντα κατὰ σάρκα γεννᾶσθαι Χριστόν. τὸν δὲ Ἡσαῦ, τύπον ἔχοντα τοῦ προτέρου λαοῦ, ϑ8δωβοῦον ϑοῦδβὴ Ὀοῦρθυ δοοῶοθο ϑοὺῦ οὐδοῦ ογδδυ. We find, moreover, that the blessed Isaac also acts similarly, and that, Jacob, his younger son, he blesses and grants him all favors because of Christ who, according to the flesh, was to be begotten of him. But Esau, type-bearer of the first people, (Isaac), knowing in some way

in advance the wicked calculation that would come into his heart, did not bless him [in his turn], but, in the strength of the term, by a prophecy, he reprimanded him. Moreover, that it is indeed so, the Scripture itself once opened will teach us more clearly what was said previously (30). Thus, indeed, it speaks: 'And Isaac called his elder son and said to him: "Take your equipment: your quiver and your bow, and go out into the plain, and kill me some game, and make me a good dish, as I (like it), and bring (it)

προειδώς που τὸν « ἐν τῇ καρδίᾳ πονηρὸν λογισμόν, τοῦτον οὐκ αὖ ηὐλόγησεν, ἀλλά, δυνάμει ῥητῶν, διὰ προφητείας ἐπέπληξεν. ὅτι δὲ οὕτως ἔχει, αὐτὴ ἡ γραφὴ ἀνα- πτυχθεῖσα σαφέστερον ἡμᾶς διδάξει τὰ προειρημένα. Π. λέγει γὰρ οὕτως" « καὶ ἐκάλε- σεν Ἰσαὰκ τὸν υἱὸν αὐτοῦ τὸν πρεσθύτερον καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ" λάθε τὸ σκεῦός σου, τήν τε φαρέτραν καὶ τὸ τόξον καὶ ἔξελθε εἰς τὸ πεδίον καὶ θήρευσόν μοι θήραν, καὶ ποίησόν μοι ἐδέσματα ὡς φιλῶ ἐγώ" καὶ ἔνεγκέ μοι, ἵνα φάγω, ὅπως εὐλογήσῃ σε ἡ ψυχή μου πρὸ τοῦ ἀποθανεῖν με ». τὸ μὲν οὖν ἐντέλλεσθαι τὸν προφήτην τροφὴν αἰτοῦντα παρὰ
to me, that I may eat (it), so that my soul may bless you before I die.' The fact, therefore, that, on one hand, the prophet, asking Esau for food, does so by giving him an order (31), signifies the call that the Word makes the first people hear when He asks them for the fruit of the works of justice, (justice) which was considered as food for the Father (32). For these words: 'Go out into the plain and kill me some game' designate life in the world. The fact, on the other hand, of saying to him: 'Take your quiver and your bow' shows that the people were to, claiming (33) their personal glory, not at all
τοῦ Ἡσαῦ σημαίνει τὴν διὰ τοῦ λόγου κλῆσιν τοῦ προτέρου λαοῦ γεγενημένην, ὡς ἀπαιτεῖ καρπὸν ἔργων δικαιοσύνης παρ᾽ αὐτοῦ, ἥτις ἦν ὡς τροφὴ τῷ πατρὶ λογιζομένη. τὸ γὰρ « ἔξελθε εἰς τὸ πεδίον καὶ θήρευσόν μοι θήραν » τὴν ἐν τῷ κόσμῳ ἀναστροφὴν σημαίνει. τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν πρὸς αὐτόν, « λάθε τὴν φαρέτραν καὶ τὸ τόξον σου» δηλοῖ, ὅτι ἔμελλεν ὁ λαὸς ἰδίας δόξης ἀντιποιούμενος μὴ πίστει δικαιοῦσθαι, ἀλλ᾽ ἐν πολέμῳ καὶ ὁθο-ὦ φῦ δῶν Θογῦν. οὥσϑοςο ΕΚ ΌΝΝ σγβο)ϑγφρονο ϑοδογδοσγο φουδοχὸ
be justified by faith, but, glorying in war and the sword, ask for a king (who will be) their tyrant (34); as Moses, he too, (was) saying to them: '... and the sword is that in which you glory (35).' We find, moreover, that the blessed Isaac also acts similarly, and that, Jacob, his younger son, he blesses and grants him all favors because of Christ who, according to the flesh, was to be begotten of him. But Esau, type-bearer of the first people, (Isaac), knowing in some way

in advance the wicked calculation that would come into his heart, did not bless him [in his turn], but, in the strength of the term, by a prophecy, he reprimanded him. Moreover, that it is indeed so, the Scripture itself once opened will teach us more clearly what was said previously (30). Thus, indeed, it speaks: 'And Isaac called his elder son and said to him: "Take your equipment: your quiver and your bow, and go out into the plain, and kill me some game, and make me a good dish, as I (like it), and bring (it) to me, that I may eat (it), so that my soul may bless you before I die.' The fact, therefore, that, on one hand, the prophet, asking Esau for food, does so by giving him an order (31), signifies the call that the Word makes the first people hear when He asks them for the fruit of the works of justice, (justice) which was considered as food for the Father (32). For these words: 'Go out into the plain and kill me some game' designate life in the world. 'Now Rebecca said to Jacob, her younger son: "Behold, I have just heard your father speaking to Esau, your brother, and saying to him: 'Bring me some of your game, and make me a dish, and, after having eaten, I will bless you before the Lord.' Now therefore, listen to my voice, my child, and, having gone (to the park) of the sheep, take me from there two small kids (36), tender and beautiful; and I will make a dish for your father, as he (likes it), and you will bring (it) to your father, and he will eat (of it); so that your father may bless you before he dies." 'Now Rebecca said to Jacob, her younger son: "Behold, I have just heard your father speaking to Esau, your brother, and saying to him: 'Bring me some of your game, and make me a dish, and, after having eaten, I will bless you before the Lord.' Now therefore, listen to my voice, my child, and, having gone (to the park) of the sheep, take me from there two small kids (36), tender and beautiful; and I will make a dish for your father, as he (likes it), and you will bring (it) to your father, and he will eat (of it); so that your father may bless you before he dies."

On one hand, therefore, Rebecca, image-bearer of the Church, was already prefiguring (there) the future which was to be realized through her younger son. For she says to him: 'Having gone (to the park) of the sheep, take me from there two small kids, tender and beautiful.' Esau, for his part, is sent into the plain as living as a stranger (37) in the world; but Jacob (is sent) (to the park) of the sheep, so that what was said by the Lord may be fulfilled: 'I have been sent only to the lost sheep of the House of Israel.' On one hand, therefore, Rebecca, image-bearer of the Church, was already prefiguring (there) the future which was to be realized through her younger son. For she says to him: 'Having gone (to the park) of the sheep, take me from there two small kids, tender and beautiful.' Esau, for his part, is sent into the plain as living as a stranger (37) in the world; but Jacob (is sent) (to the park) of the sheep, so that what was said by the Lord may be fulfilled: 'I have been sent only to the lost sheep of the House of Israel.'


[p. 16] {#po.27.p16}
Τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν αὐτήν, ὡλάδε μοι ἐκεῖθεν δύο ἐρίφους ἁπαλοὺς καὶ καλοὺς » ἔδειξεν τὰς δύο κλήσεις, τὰς διὰ τοῦ εὐαγγελίου γεγενημένας" ἔριφοι μὲν ὄντες ἀπ᾽ ἀρχῆς, ἐπειδὴ πάντες ἦμεν ἁμαρτωλοί, ἁπαλοὶ δὲ καὶ καλοὶ διὰ ὑπακοῆς γινόμεθα τῇ ἐν Χριστῷ πίστει δικαιούμενοι, μηκέτι ὡς ἔριφοι κρινόμενοι, ἀλλ᾽ ὡς πρόδατα εἰς « ὀσμὴν εὐωδίας » θυσίᾳ καθαρᾷ θεῷ προσφερόμενοι, « καὶ εἰς τροφὴν πνευματικὴν τῷ λόγῳ ἐξηρτισμένοι "- ὃς διὰ τοῦ εὐαγγελίου τὰ ὅμοια ἐπιτελῶν ἔλεγεν τοῖς μαθηταῖς" « ἐγὼ βρῶσιν ἔχω φαγεῖν, ἣν ὑμεῖς οὐκ οἴδατε ». φῦ ὥωϑος» οδο ϑοῦ. ϑοογνῶϑοτο ϑωδϑδ. Τ' " ὁσδ ὃ αὖδο ογοβοδῦο
On the other hand, by the fact that she said: 'Take me from there two small kids, tender and beautiful,' she indicated (thereby) the two calls which, in fact, are found to have been addressed by the Gospel. On one hand, being kids at the beginning (38), since we were all sinners, we become on the other hand, through obedience, tender and beautiful (39), justified by faith in Christ, no longer as condemned kids but, as sheep, in pure sacrifice, in 'odor of sweetness' offered to God <and constituted spiritual food for the Word> (40) who, by means of the Gospel, giving to the similitudes their full realization, said to his disciples: 'I have, myself, a food to eat, which you, you do not know.'

But Jacob said to Rebecca his mother: 'My brother is (41) a hairy man, and I, a hairless man. (I fear) that my father may come to feel me, and I will appear (42) in his eyes to despise him, and I will draw upon myself a curse and not a blessing.' How (43) clearly here Jacob has indeed shown his own piety! For instructed in what had happened through the fault of Ham, who, having seen Noah naked, had mocked the nakedness of his father, and had drawn upon himself the curse, he took good care not to go and risk himself too (44) of passing for someone

trying to deceive his own father and to fall under a curse. Furthermore, what is said (there) by him was true also, namely: 'Esau my brother, he is a hairy man,' that is to say, a sinner, 'but I am, myself, a hairless man,' by which is shown (the character) that the flesh of the Lord has of being without stain and without sin. But since it was not necessary that the mysteries, on another (than the Lord? Jacob?), be accomplished (45), (Rebecca) said to him (46): 'Upon me (be) your curse, my child (47); only be docile to my voice, and go, bring me'

Καὶ γὰρ ἔστιν ἐπιγνῶναι τὸ εἰρη μένον ὑπὸ τῆς 'Ρεδέκκας πάλαι, νῦν ἐπὶ τὴν ἐκ- κλησίαν πεπληρωμένον. τὸ γὰρ « ἐπ᾽ ἐμὲ ἡ κατάρα σου τέκνον », τοῦτο δηλοῖ, ὅπερ νῦν τινες βλασφημοῦντες τὴν ἐκκλησίαν ὀνειδίζουσιν ὡς σεδομένην τὸν ἐσταυρωμένον, κατάραν ἡμῖν καὶ ὄνειδος κατὰ ταῦτα ἐπιφέροντες. τὸ γὰρ πάθος τοῦ κυρίου τοῖς μὲν ἀπίστοις νομίζεται χατάρα, τοῖς δὲ πιστεύουσιν « ζωὴ καὶ εἰρήνη ». καὶ γὰρ ὁ ἀπόστολος λέγει' « Χριστὸς ἡμᾶς ἐξηγόρασεν ἐκ τῆς κατάρας τοῦ νόμου, γενόμενος ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν κατάρα», ὅπερ ἐπεπλέρωσεν ὁ οὐ ὁῶν δηφροῦ-ὁθοῦ- οι σὴς» σοϑοςρο οδο ὥρδηροοῦ ϑορῶ βοδοουβοῶ,
For one can recognize that what, formerly, was said by Rebecca has now been accomplished upon the Church. For: 'Upon me (be) your curse, my child,' this makes manifest what precisely some now, in their blasphemies, outrageously reproach the Church for, of venerating the Crucified, adding on this subject against us curse and outrage (48). For (49) the Passion of the Lord is, on one hand, for the infidels, reputed a curse, on the other, for the faithful 'life and peace.' And, indeed, the apostle says: 'Christ redeemed us from the curse of the Law, having become a curse for us,' which is precisely what

the Savior has now accomplished (50) having, thanks to his body, taken upon Himself, on the wood, the death <of man> (51), in order, by His own obedience, to abolish the curse of Adam laid in the Law (52), namely: 'You are earth and to earth you will return.' 'Rebecca therefore, having taken the robe of her elder son, the most beautiful, clothed her son Jacob in it and put around his arms the skins of the small kids.' [On one hand] (53), the fact of clothing him with the robe signifies that the Word was to clothe Himself with flesh; on the other hand, the fact of putting the skins of the small kids around his arms shows that He took upon Himself the sins of us all, once He had extended His hands and His arms on the cross, as Isaiah also said: 'He Himself took upon Himself our sins and bore our infirmities.' [On one hand] (53), the fact of clothing him with the robe signifies that the Word was to clothe Himself with flesh; on the other hand, the fact of putting the skins of the small kids around his arms shows that He took upon Himself the sins of us all, once He had extended His hands and His arms on the cross, as Isaiah also said: 'He Himself took upon Himself our sins and bore our infirmities.'

The fact, moreover, that Jacob says to his father: 'I have done one by one the things that you had told me to do' signifies the obedience in which the Word stood day by day (54) toward His Father; as also indeed in Ezekiel He says: 'I did one by one the things that He had ordered me to do.' The fact, moreover, that Jacob says to his father: 'I have done one by one the things that you had told me to do' signifies the obedience in which the Word stood day by day (54) toward His Father; as also indeed in Ezekiel He says: 'I did one by one the things that He had ordered me to do.'
Ἐΐτον δὲ αὐτῷ Ἰσαάκ: ε τές οἵ σύ; ὁ δὲ οἴκεν' ἐγώ εἰμι υἱὲς σου ὁ πρωτότοκος ᾿Ησόδ. οὐχ ὡς ἀγνοῶν ὁ προφήτης ἐπερωτᾷ τὸν Ἰακώδ᾽ ἐνορᾷ γὰρ πνευματικῶς τὰ γινόμενα. οὐ γὰρ ᾿ἸΙησοῦς ὁ τοῦ Ναυῆ ἐπερωτῶν τοὺς Γαδαωνίτας ἠγνόει τὸ ὑπ᾽ αὐτῶν τετεχνασμένον πανούρ- γευμα, ἀλλὰ εἰδότως συνεχώρει, ἵνα τὰ ἔθνη εἰς Ἰησοῦν καὶ εἰς ᾿Τησοῦν μετανοοῦντα καὶ πιστεύοντα σωθῇ. οὕτως οὖν καὶ ἐνθάδε νοεῖν χρή. Now Isaac said to him (to Jacob): 'Who are you?' He said to him: 'I am your son, your first-born Esau (55).' It is not because he is ignorant that the prophet questions Jacob; for he [contemplates] in their spiritual significance the events. Joshua, son of Nun, questioning the Gibeonites was not indeed ignorant of what in their deceit they had schemed, but [knowingly] he pardoned, so that [the Gentiles] coming to repentance and believing in Joshua and in Jesus might be saved. Thus, therefore, here also (in Genesis), must one understand (56).

"Come near to me," said (Isaac), "and I will feel you, (my) child, (to see) if you are my son Esau, or not." [He] approached him. And he, after having felt him, said: "The voice, indeed, is the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau." "Come near to me," said (Isaac), "and I will feel you, (my) child, (to see) if you are my son Esau, or not." And he approached him. And he, after having felt him, said: "The voice, indeed, is the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau."

This signifies that (57) the Word, the one who is in Jacob prefiguring the mysteries, also became the voice of the prophets, himself making them see the future in advance (58). But his hands became "hands of Esau," for, because of the sins of the people, he was delivered to death. Φησὶν οὖν πρὸς αὐτόν’ « ἔγγισόν μοι, τέκνον, καὶ φίλησόν με. καὶ ἐγγίσας ἐφίλη- 'σεν αὐτόν. καὶ ὠσφράνθη τὴν ὀσμὴν τῶν ἱματίων αὐτοῦ, καὶ ηὐλόγησεν αὐτὸν καὶ εἶπεν ». καὶ τοῦτο σαφῶς προμηνύων ὁ προφήτης, ὡς οὐδεὶς ἀνθρώπων στόμα ἅγιον προσοίσει πατρὶ ἣ μόνος ὁ πρωτότοκος ἐχ τῆς παρθένου γενόμενος « υἱός "». « ἐγὼ γάρ φησιν εἰμὶ ὁ υἱός σου, ὁ πρωτότοκος ». παραυτίκα γοῦν ἐπήνεγκεν τὴν εὐλογίαν ὁ προφήτης καὶ λέγει" « ἰδοὺ ὀσμὴ τῶν ἱματίων τοῦ υἱοῦ μου « ὡς Ξ- ὀσμὴ ἀγροῦ πλήρους, ὃν εὐλόγησε κύριος" καὶ δῴη σοι ὁ θεὸς ἀπὸ τῆς δρόσου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ καὶ ἀπὸ τῆς πιότητος τῆς γῆς πλῆθος σίτου καὶ οἴνου" καὶ δουλευσάτωσάν σοι .« ἔθνη, καὶ προσκυνήσουσίν σοι Σ-- ἄρχοντες, καὶ γίνου κύριος τοῦ ἀδελφοῦ σου, καὶ προσκυνήσουσίν σε οἱ υἱοὶ τοῦ πατρός σου. ὁ καταρώμενός σε ἐπιχα- τάρατος, ὁ δὲ εὐλογῶν σε εὐλογημένος ».
He (Isaac) said to him therefore: "Come near to me, (my) child, and kiss me." And, having approached, he (Jacob) kissed him. And he (Isaac) smelled the odor of his garments and blessed him and said. And, saying this, the prophet clearly reveals in advance that no one among men will offer the Father a holy mouth, except, and alone, the firstborn <son> (58bis), born of the Virgin. "I am," said (Jacob) in effect, "your son, the firstborn." Immediately therefore the prophet imposed his blessing upon him and said: "Behold the odor of the garments of my son, <like> (59) the odor of a fertile field which the Lord has blessed! And may God give you, of the dew of heaven and of the fat of the earth, abundance of grain and wine! And may the <Gentiles> be enslaved to you, <and may> the princes <worship you> (60)! And be the lord of your brother, and may the sons of your father worship you! Whoever curses you, let him be cursed! Whoever blesses you, let him be blessed!"

favor before his face; after that, again (62) (one finds him) descending into Egypt, so as not to perish of famine with his children. Upon whom, therefore, is what is said there accomplished: "Behold the odor of the garments of my son, like the odor of a fertile field which the Lord has blessed"? Upon no one other than upon Christ, the Son of God. For the field is the world. And the odor of his garments are all those who believe in Him, as the Apostle says: "We are the good odor of Christ among those who are saved (63) and among those who are perishing; to the latter indeed an odor of death, for death; to the former, an odor of life, for life (64)." If truly, therefore, someone estimates that this blessing was realized upon Jacob, he is in error. For [nothing of all this] happened to Jacob (61). For one finds him first in Mesopotamia, twenty years in the service of Laban, then, in person, worshipping his own brother Esau and, by presents, seeking to find

favor before his face; after that, again (62) (one finds him) descending into Egypt, so as not to perish of famine with his children. Upon whom, therefore, is what is said there accomplished: "Behold the odor of the garments of my son, like the odor of a fertile field which the Lord has blessed"? Upon no one other than upon Christ, the Son of God. For the field is the world. And the odor of his garments are all those who believe in Him, as the Apostle says: "We are the good odor of Christ among those who are saved (63) and among those who are perishing; to the latter indeed an odor of death, for death; to the former, an odor of life, for life (64)."


[p. 22] {#po.27.p22}
ἔτη δουλεύων τῷ Λάβαν, ἔπειτα αὐτὸς τὸν ἴδιον ἀδελφὸν Ἡσαῦ προσκυνῶν καὶ διὰ τῶν δώρων τὸ τούτου πρόσωπον ἐξιλασκόμενος, μετὰ ταῦτα πάλιν εἰς Αἴγυπτον κατερχόμενος, ἵνα μὴ σὺν τοῖς τέκνοις λιμῷ διαφθαρῇ. ἐπὶ τίνα οὖν πληροῦται τὸ εἰρημένον" « ἰδοὺ ὀσμὴ τῶν ἱματίων τοῦ υἱοῦ μου’ ὧς ὀσμὴ ἀγροῦ πλήρης, ὃν εὐλόγησεν κύριος»; ἐπ᾽ οὐδένα ἄλλον, εἰ μὴ ἐπὶ τὸν Χριστόν, τὸν υἱὸν τοῦ θεοῦ. ἀγρὸς γάρ ἐστιν ὁ κόσμος. ὀσμὴ δὲ τῶν ἱματίων αὐτοῦ εἰσιν πάντες οἱ πιστεύοντες εἰς αὐτόν. ὡς ὁ ἀπόστολος λέγει" « ὅτι Χριστοῦ εὐωδία ἐσμὲν ἐν τοῖς σωζομένοις καὶ ἐν τοῖς ἀπολλυμένοις, οἷς μὲν γὰρ ὀσμὴ θανάτου εἰς θάνατον, οἷς δὲ ὀσμὴ ζωῆς εἰς ζωήν ». ογος δο- οὐ ϑρραδοογὸ δοῦδ ᾿ϑοδῦ» ϑϑδοῶν. φοὁ ὁϑοῦο ϑοϑοοδώϑοςυ ἡ γϑόθοο ϑογόϑοςου. ὥδωογὺ δῶν δοϑο,δοο)- Ὀδομϑοφροογ ϑωϑωςοϑδ οῦ 8δοὺ ὕφῳυ δροῦ- Φωςροδοῦ σγφογϑῶφρο οδο. 8οογοῦϑες δ᾽ Ὀφηφρδρφροὅδο δου βοϑοῦσα ογοῶῦβὺ Ὀεγφρο ὁδουβοδὸ Ὀοϑοῦοω, Οωώϑῆφρο ὁ ε)ο)δὺ ὠχρροφοϑοδ, οσβοῦ Ὅρφου. οὥσϑος δουσώρτυ ὕροου ϑοῦυ ϑοῦ οϑῶογοῦο ὁδοῶδα ὁ Ὀώρηοφρο δώ φηδ5- φορδοδο ϑοὺῦ ϑωϊδορδοῦο ϑοδοῦοδο οὥοοδ “αϑοςρο ὥωϑοφρο ϑβοδὺ οδο. οοῶρβο ϑαβοΐηςρο οὐὗο οὐδε). ϑοσγοῶϑοςυ ὃ.» Τ Ὀσγφρδοσροδοῦ δθουθοῦο. οδωβοςρονῦ τοῦ ὃρωφουῦο)ο. As for this saying: "May God give you, of the dew of heaven and of the fat of the earth, abundance of grain and wine," very clearly, by the term (employed), he designated the Word, who descended from heaven like dew; and, by the earth, the flesh which he assumed from the Virgin; and, by saying: "Abundance of grain and wine," he showed the saints gathered like grain (65) in a storehouse and, thanks to the Spirit, [justified as by wine] (66). As for this saying: "May God give you, of the dew of heaven and of the fat of the earth, abundance of grain and wine," very clearly, by the term (employed), he designated the Word, who descended from heaven like dew; and, by the earth, the flesh which he assumed from the Virgin; and, by saying: "Abundance of grain and wine," he showed the saints gathered like grain (65) in a storehouse and, thanks to the Spirit, [justified as by wine] (66).

Τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν’ « δουλουσάτωσάν σοι ἔθνη καὶ προσκυνήσουσίν σοι ἄρχοντες » «“ τοῦτο καὶ μεχρὶ νῦν γίνεται" τίνι σήμερον τὰ ἔθνη πιστεύοντα δουλεύουσι καὶ τίνα προσκυνοῦσιν οἱ τῆς ἐκκλησίας ἄρχοντες 5» ἀλλ᾽ ἢ τὸν Χριστόν, δι’ οὗ ὀνόματος καὶ σώζονται. ὡς καὶ διὰ Ἡσαΐου αὐτὸς ὁ λόγος προαναφωνῶν λέγει « τοῖς δὲ δουλεύουσίν μοι κληθήσεται ὄνομα καινόν, ὃ εὐλογηθήσεται ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς" εὐλογήσουσιν γὰρ τὸν θεὸν τὸν ἀληθινὸν « καὶ οἱ ὁμνύοντες ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς ὁμοῦνται τὸν θεὸν τὸν ἀληθινόν "- ». καὶ πάλιν λέγει" «ἰδοὺ οἱ δουλεύοντές μοι φάγονται, ὑμεῖς δὲ πεινάσετε᾽ ἰδοὺ οἱ δουλεύοντές μοι πίονται, ὑμεῖς δὲ διψήσετε᾽ ἰδοὺ οἱ δουλεύοντές μοι ἀγαλλιάσονται ἐν εὐφροσύνῃ, ὑμεῖς δὲ αἰσχυνθήσεσθε, ἀπὸ συντριδῆς πνεύματος ὀλολύξετε ». φοῦ ὥσϑ)0). σγή)5. ϑ8οσγοῶϑοφο ὁϑωδοδςοῦδ Ὅο6 δοσοδοϑδο «ςοὺ ογομεδοῦδϑόϑοοοῦ
As for this saying: "May the Gentiles be enslaved to you! And may the princes worship you!" <this, even until this hour, is being realized. Who, today, do the Gentiles, having become believers, serve? And whom do the princes of the Church worship> (67), if not Christ by whose name they are also saved? As also, through Isaiah, the Word himself, announcing it in advance, says: "Those who serve me will be called by a new name, which will be blessed on the earth. For they will bless the true God; <and those who swear by the heavens, swear (henceforth) by the true God> (68)." And again he says: "Behold those who serve me will eat; but you, you will be hungry; behold those who serve me will drink; but you, you will be thirsty; behold those who serve me will exult with joy; but you, you will be in shame; in the tearing of your spirit you will howl."

said there has its fulfillment in the Savior. For of those who are regarded as being, according to the flesh, his brothers, he became Lord and Master (69), so that, by them, as King, he might be worshipped. It is for this that he says: "Whoever curses you, let him be cursed! But whoever blesses you, let him be blessed!" Then he adds, saying: "Be the lord of your brother; and may the sons of your father worship you." In truth, for Jacob, both are false: no one worshipped him and he was not lord of his brother Esau; but, quite the contrary, he fled from him in fear, and, first, worshipped him seven times. What, therefore, is

Without any doubt, the dish of Esau designates the worship of the people under the law, in that (this people) boasted and estimated being justified by circumcision. They offered (to God) the Gentile proselytes by way of food, lacking food themselves, not being able to touch the heavenly bread (71). Without any doubt, the dish of Esau designates the worship of the people under the law, in that (this people) boasted and estimated being justified by circumcision. They offered (to God) the Gentile proselytes by way of food, lacking food themselves, not being able to touch the heavenly bread (71).
ΡΟ ρ. «-ἘπΠ- Εἴπεν οὖν Ἡσαῦ" « ἀναστήτω ὁ πατήρ μου καὶ φαγέτω τῆς θήρας τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, ὅπως εὐλογήσῃ με ἡ ψυχή σου ». πῶς ὑπερήφανον τὸ ῥῆμα καὶ ἐπηρμένον τῇ καρδίᾳ" καὶ γὰρ ἕως σήμερον οἱ ἐκ περιτομῆς ὁμοίως καυχῶνται, ὡς μόνοι δεδικαιωμένοι. ὁ δὲ μακάριος Ἰακὼδ τὸ εὐλαδὲς καὶ ταπεινόφρον προσλαδόμενος ἔλεγεν τῷ πατρί" « ἐποίησα καθ᾽ ἃ ἐλάλησάς μοι»" ὁ δὲ ᾿Ησαῦ οὐχ οὕτως, ἀλλὰ « ἀναστήτω ὁ πατήρ μου καὶ φαγέτω τῆς θήρας τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, ὅπως εὐλογήσῃ με ἡ ψυχή σου ». ὁ δὲ πρὸς αὐτὸν ἔφη" «τίς εἴ σύ; ὁ δὲ εἴπεν' ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ υἱός σου, ὁ πρωτότοκος, ᾿Ησαῦ. ἐξέστη δὲ ᾿Ισαὰκ ἔκστασιν. μεγάλην σφόδρα καὶ εἴπεν᾽ τίς οὖν ὁ θηρεύσας μοι θήραν καὶ εἰσενέγκας μοι, καὶ ἔφαγον ἀπὸ πάντων πρὸ τοῦ σε ἐλθεῖν καὶ ηὐλόγησα αὐτόν, καὶ εὐλογημένος ἔσται ». πῶς ἐγρηγόρησεν ἐνθάδε τὸ πνεῦμα τοῦ προφήτου. ἐπειδὴ γὰρ ἐν τῇ εὐλογίᾳ προσομιλῶν τῷ Ἰακὼδ εἴπεν᾽ « ὁ εὐλογῶν σε εὐλογημένος" ὁ δὲ καταρώμενός σε ἐπικατάρατος», ΄ διὰ τοῦτο ἐπεσφαλίσατο ἐκ δευτέ- ρου καὶ ἐπεσφράγισεν τὴν πρώτην εὐλογίαν εἰπών' « ηὐλόγησα αὐτὸν καὶ εὐλογημένος ἔσται »ν. ἦν γὰρ τὸ γεγονὸς ὑπὸ τοῦ Ἰακὼδ μυστήριον οἰκονομίας εἰς Χριστὸν προτυπού- μενον, ὃς εὐλογημένος ὑπὸ τοῦ πατρὸς γεννηθεὶς εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας ἐγένετο εὐλογημένος. ὁρφῳοϑὸ ϑοϑοῶ βοϑω φὺ ΕΝ δυςοοῶοῦυ δὺδ θοῦδὸ δϑοδοῦσα τοὺ
Esau therefore said: "Let my father arise and eat of the hunt of his son; so that your soul may bless me." What pride in these words! And what arrogance in this heart! And, in effect, until today, those of the circumcision boast similarly, alone as they are, they think, to be justified. The blessed Jacob, for his part, joining to it piety and humility of spirit, said to his father: "I have done one by one (the things) that you told me (to do)." Esau, for his part, nothing of the sort, but: "Let my father arise and eat of the hunt of his son; so that your soul may bless me." Now (Isaac) said to him: "Who are you?" (Esau) said: "I am your son, your firstborn, Esau." Then, beside himself, Isaac was greatly, very strongly, ecstatic, and said: "Who is it then who went to hunt for me game and brought it to me; and I ate of everything before you came; and I blessed him, and blessed he shall be." How here the spirit of the prophet remained awake! Because in effect, in his blessing, addressing Jacob, he had said: "Whoever blesses you, let him be blessed! But whoever curses you, let him be cursed!" For this, he confirmed a second time and sealed the first blessing by saying: "I have blessed him, and blessed he shall be." What in effect had happened by the fact of the act of Jacob was a mystery of (divine) economy in view of Christ prefigured (by Jacob), (of Christ) who, blessed by the Father, once born (temporal birth), was blessed for the ages.

Τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν τὴν γραφήν" « ἐξέστη δὲ Ἰσαὰκ “- ἔκστασιν μεγάλην σφόδρα », τοῦτ᾽ ἔστιν" ἐθαύμασεν Ἰσαὰκ ἐπὶ τῷ γεγονότι, προορῶν τὰ "- ἔθνη διὰ τοῦ νεωτέρου υἱοῦ ὑπὸ "- τοῦ θεοῦ μέλλοντα εὐλογεῖσθαι καὶ εἰς τὴν διαθήκην τῆς ἐπαγγελίας τῶν πατέρων χωρεῖν. διὰ τοῦτο γὰρ καὶ Ἰακὼδ τικτόμενος καὶ ἔσχατος ἐκ κοιλίας μητρὸς ἐκπορευόμενος τὴν πτέρναν ἐκράτει τοῦ Ἡσαῦ" ἤμελλεν γὰρ ὁ τοῖς ἴχνεσιν τῶν προφητῶν χατακολουθῶν ἔσχατος λαὸς λήψεσθαι τὰ πρωτοτόκια εἰς καινὴν διαθήκην πρῶτος εὑρισκόμενος. δῶοδγω Ουϑ ον οὐ» θοΐος»ο οδο. δοογοῦϑοφο φοὐτ ςου οὐ ϑ φοὐφπῶ58)--
For what the Scripture says: "Then, beside himself, Isaac <was greatly, very strongly, ecstatic,> it is the equivalent of: "Isaac was seized with admiration> (72)" regarding what had happened, seeing in advance that, the Gentiles, it was through the younger son that, <by> (73) God, they were to be blessed and access the testament of the Promise made to the Fathers. It is for this, in effect, that Jacob still, at the moment when he was being born and coming out last from the womb of his mother, was clutching the heel of Esau. It is that in effect, following closely in the trace of the steps of the prophets, the last people were to seize the birthright, first that one finds it in the New Testament (74).

The fact, therefore, of saying to him: "Your <brother>, having come with deceit, <has taken your blessing>" (75) signifies, implying there a mystery (76), that the Word of God,

once incarnate, was to take the form of a slave so that, [thanks to him (the slave), unknown] in his generation (temporal) (77), he might receive the blessing of the Father, communicating it to us also, we who believe in him. The fact, therefore, of saying to him: "Your <brother>, having come with deceit, <has taken your blessing>" (75) signifies, implying there a mystery (76), that the Word of God,

Now Esau, once this was heard, said: "Quite justly he was called by the name of Jacob! For he had already (78) supplanted me! This time it is the second! My birthright he has already taken! And now he comes to take my blessing!" And Esau remained deeply irritated against Jacob. And he said, saying it in his heart: "When will they approach (79) the days when I will mourn for my father? So that I may kill Jacob, my brother!" Now Esau, once this was heard, said: "Quite justly he was called by the name of Jacob! For he had already (78) supplanted me! This time it is the second! My birthright he has already taken! And now he comes to take my blessing!" And Esau remained deeply irritated against Jacob. And he said, saying it in his heart: "When will they approach (79) the days when I will mourn for my father? So that I may kill Jacob, my brother!"

How manifestly here the Scripture has predicted <through Esau the future events jointly arrived>! And, in effect, the Word "Father of the people" was there shown, (Word) who, in the last times, thanks to the flesh (that he had taken) approached, until touching it, the Passion (80). As Moses, making reproaches to the people, says: "Is this what you render to the Lord? Is He not, your Father?" The Scripture has therefore distinguished so that we understand well what was said there. For the fact that Esau says: "When will they approach the days when I will mourn for my father?" is explained by the days of the Passover,

during which the Word [was approaching], preaching to them the Kingdom (81). But that Esau says: "So that I may kill Jacob, my brother," it is so that (thereby) might be shown the man himself (in the Word), the man who, according to the flesh, was begotten of Jacob, and, by him (Esau = the Jewish people) nailed to the wood. How manifestly here the Scripture has predicted <through Esau the future events jointly arrived>! And, in effect, the Word "Father of the people" was there shown, (Word) who, in the last times, thanks to the flesh (that he had taken) approached, until touching it, the Passion (80). As Moses, making reproaches to the people, says: "Is this what you render to the Lord? Is He not, your Father?" The Scripture has therefore distinguished so that we understand well what was said there. For the fact that Esau says: "When will they approach the days when I will mourn for my father?" is explained by the days of the Passover,

Esau (82) said to him (Isaac) then: "Have you not, besides, left a blessing for me, father?" Indeed, so that the whole fullness of the divinity might be found dwelling bodily in Christ, the latter (83) (Isaac) said to him: "If I have made him your Lord, and made all his brothers his servants, I have (at the same time) consolidated upon him (alone) the rights to the grain and the oil; from then on, for you, what can I do, child? (84)"

"Before the breaking (85) of Isaac, Esau let out a great cry and wept." The breaking of Isaac, then, is the pity which, down to the very bowels, moved the Word before the transgression (86) of the people. And, indeed, the Savior, praying for them, said: "Father (87), forgive them, for they do not know what they are doing."

Χ. ἀλλ᾽ ἐπειδὴ καιρὸς οὐκ ἦν, προφητεύων Ἰσαὰκ τὰ μέλλοντα φησὶν πρὸς αὐτόν’ «ἰδοὺ ἀπὸ τῆς πιότητος « τῆς "- γῆς ἔσται ἡ κατοίκησίς σου καὶ ἀπὸ τῆς δρόσου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ἄνωθεν καὶ ἐπὶ τῇ μαχαίρᾳ σου ζήσει καὶ τῷ ἀδελφῷ σου δουλεύσεις" ἔσται δὲ ἡνίκα ἂν καθέλῃς καὶ ἐκλύσῃς τὸν ζυγὸν ἀπὸ τοῦ τραχήλου σου ». τὰ μὲν εἰρημένα ὑπὸ τοῦ μακαρίου ᾿Ιδαὰκ πότερον εὐλογίας ἔχει δύναμιν ἢἣ προφητείας; χρὴ γὰρ νοεῖν τὰ προκείμενα. ἰδού φησιν᾽ « ἀπὸ τῆς πιότητος τῆς γῆς ἔσται « ἡ Σ- κατοίκησίς σου, καὶ ἀπὸ τῆς δρόσου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ἄνωθεν ». συνέθη γὰρ χατοικῆσαι τὸν λαὸν ἐν τῇ γῇ τῶν Χαναναίων, « ἣν Σ» διένειμεν αὐτοῖς Ἰησοῦς ὁ τοῦ Ναυῇ. δῇ οὔρ δῶν ονῦρ8). οὗο. ὥωδῃςοῖλο δϑορῦςου θοδοδςορφοδὸ “δὺο
That was, then, the blessing that Esau, at that time, was asking to receive. But, because it was not the time, Isaac, prophesying the future, said to him: "Behold, from the fatness of the earth shall your dwelling be, and from the dew that descends from heaven (94). And by your sword you shall live, and of your brother you shall be a servant. Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." Truly, does what the blessed Isaac says there have the value of a blessing or of a prophecy? For one must well understand what precedes. Behold, he says: "From the fatness of the earth shall your dwelling be, and from the dew that descends from heaven." It happened, indeed, that the people settled in the land of the Canaanites which Joshua, son of Nun, divided among them. And that Isaac says: "and from the

dew that descends from heaven" is because the prophets, like a cloud, left them soaked with dew (95) after having made known to them (96) the oracles of God. And that he says: "By your sword you shall live" is because, always, the people did not cease to be in aggressive and defensive war against the peoples established all around them, as the Scriptures themselves show. And what he says: "And of your brother you shall be a servant" marks the epoch which still lasts now, (an epoch) where the Savior was present, having come to visit his own brothers according to the flesh, (the Savior) whom the prophet (here) advises, after one has become (97) docile, to serve. It is for this that he said: "Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." What yoke if not the yoke that lies in the Law? So that they no longer live now as slaves under the yoke of the Law, but that, as free men believing in the Gospel, they may now still be saved (98).
τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν « καὶ ἀπὸ τῆς ϑρόσου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ἄνωθεν », ὅτι οἱ προφῆται νεφέλης δέπκην ἐδρόσιζον αὐτοὺς γνωρίσαντες αὐτοῖς τὰ τοῦ θεοῦ λόγια. τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν « ἐπὶ τῇ μαχαίρᾳ σου ζήσει », ὅτι ἀεὶ ὁ λαὸς οὐκ ἐπαύσατο πολεμῶν καὶ πολεμούμενος πρὸς τὰ κύκλωθεν ἔθνη κατοικοῦντα, καθὼς αὐταὶ αἱ γραφαὶ δηλοῦσιν. τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν « καὶ τῷ ἀδελφῷ σου δουλεύσεις » σημαίνει τὸν νῦν ἐνεστῶτα καιρόν, ἐν ᾧ παρῆν ὁ σωτὴρ ἐπισκεψάμενος τοὺς ἰδίους κατὰ σάρκα ἀδελφούς, ᾧ παραινεῖ ὁ προφήτης ὑπήκοον γενάμενον δουλεύειν. διὰ τοῦτο ἔφη « ἔσται δὲ ἡνίκα ἂν καθέλῃς καὶ ἐκλύσῃς τὸν ζυγὸν ἀπὸ τοῦ τραχήλου σου ». ποῖον ζυγόν, ἀλλ᾽ ἢ τὸν ἐν τῷ νόμῳ κείμενον; ἵνα μηκέτι νῦν δοῦλοι ὑπὸ ζυγὸν νόμου πολιτεύωνται, ἀλλὰ ἐλεύθεροι τῷ εὐαγγελίῳ πιστεύοντες καὶ νῦν σωθῆναι δύνωνται. ρροῦοοῦο. ὥ»δ,ο). ϑοδοοθοῶδηδοςθονῦο οδο ϑοσοῶβο φγεγδφροῦο .θ Ὁ. ὁὀδῶφϑω “ηὔρϑοῦςουῦ ὕρφου Ὀοδεθοδὸ ϑοῦ φοδῶογοδοῦο ὕωφρα ὥωϑραν» οδο σγήγ5. ϑοογοῦϑοςυ ϑοῦχανοον» ϑηδοον Ὀρδωδδςοσδοζοοα. ὥοϑ,σ). ϑοῶσςοοῦ οδὸ δῶν φυὐρδθοδοςου ϑῳαδοῳω ον δῶϑθωφοοςο δοφϑαοῦ δοογηἶδδ80)5 Θωδοςφοῦο φοοδρ»οῶρδιης» οὐδοῦ. ϑοοοῶρ» θοδῦδο ὀγβηηφδοῦφδ τοῦ ὥωϑος» οδοὸ ογήηυ. ϑοογοῦϑεςο ϑϑοδο ϑοδθυ ϑϑωιδοῦςον. δα θοϑο δ850 ογχδ. dew that descends from heaven" is because the prophets, like a cloud, left them soaked with dew (95) after having made known to them (96) the oracles of God. And that he says: "By your sword you shall live" is because, always, the people did not cease to be in aggressive and defensive war against the peoples established all around them, as the Scriptures themselves show. And what he says: "And of your brother you shall be a servant" marks the epoch which still lasts now, (an epoch) where the Savior was present, having come to visit his own brothers according to the flesh, (the Savior) whom the prophet (here) advises, after one has become (97) docile, to serve. It is for this that he said: "Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." What yoke if not the yoke that lies in the Law? So that they no longer live now as slaves under the yoke of the Law, but that, as free men believing in the Gospel, they may now still be saved (98).

After having thus first given these indications on the blessings pronounced by Isaac, let us come also to the blessings (102) which are found in fact to have been, by Jacob himself, addressed to his sons. Thus, indeed, says the Scripture: "Now Israel, at the sight of the sons of Joseph, said: "Who are these?" (Joseph) said: "These are my sons, whom God has given me here." And (Jacob) said: "Bring (103) them to me, that I may bless them." Now the eyes of Jacob had become heavy because of old age, and could (104) no longer see well. And (Joseph) brought them near to him, and he kissed them and held them in his arms (105). And Israel said to Joseph: "Behold, I have not been deprived of (seeing) your face, and behold, God has shown me even your lineage!" And Joseph withdrew them gently <from between

the knees of (Jacob), and they worshiped him face to the ground. And Joseph having taken> (106) his two sons, Ephraim at his right, therefore at the left of Israel, and Manasseh at his left, therefore at the right of Israel, brought them near to (Jacob). Now Israel, having stretched out his right hand, placed it on the head of Ephraim—now this one was the younger—and his left hand on the head of Manasseh. Having thus crossed his hands, he blessed them and said: "O God, before whom my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, were pleasing, O God who has been nourishing me from my youth until the day we are in, O Angel who delivered me from all my evils, bless (107) these little children, and upon them shall remain imposed my name and the name of my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, and may they multiply into a great multitude on the earth!"
τὰς χεῖρας ηὐλόγησεν αὐτοὺς καὶ εἴπεν᾽ ὁ θεός, ᾧ εὐηρέστησαν οἱ πατέρες μου ἐναντίον αὐτοῦ, ᾿Αθραὰμ καὶ Ἰσαάκ, ὁ θεὸς ὁ τρέφων με ἐκ νεότητός μου ἕως τῆς σήμερον ἡμέρας, ὁ ἄγγελος, ὁ ῥυόμενός με ἐκ πάντων τῶν κακῶν μου, εὐλόγησον τὰ παιδία ταῦτα, καὶ ἐπιτεθήσεται τὸ ὄνομά μου ἐπ᾽ αὐτούς: καὶ τὸ ὄνομα τῶν πατέρων μου ᾿Αδραὰμ καὶ ᾿Ισαὰκ καὶ πληθυνθείησαν εἰς πλῆθος πολὺ ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς». the knees of (Jacob), and they worshiped him face to the ground. And Joseph having taken> (106) his two sons, Ephraim at his right, therefore at the left of Israel, and Manasseh at his left, therefore at the right of Israel, brought them near to (Jacob). Now Israel, having stretched out his right hand, placed it on the head of Ephraim—now this one was the younger—and his left hand on the head of Manasseh. Having thus crossed his hands, he blessed them and said: "O God, before whom my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, were pleasing, O God who has been nourishing me from my youth until the day we are in, O Angel who delivered me from all my evils, bless (107) these little children, and upon them shall remain imposed my name and the name of my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, and may they multiply into a great multitude on the earth!"

In saying this, the prophet has openly made us see a very great mystery. For in the fact of having crossed his hands and made (thus) the younger of the sons of Joseph, I mean Ephraim, pass to his right, but Manasseh, the elder, to his left, he has, by them, shown that there existed two calls and two peoples;

and that the younger, he, thanks to faith, is found at the right of Christ, while the older people, those who glory in the Law, are, by mutation, placed at the left. Whence Joseph, not having understood (108) in its spiritual significance what was being done there by the prophet, thinking that his father was mistaken (109), stopped him (110), and said: "Not so, father! For this one is the firstborn! Put your right (hand) on his head." But Jacob said: "I know, my child; he also will be great, he also will be exalted, but his brother, the younger, will be greater than he." And he placed (111) Ephraim before Manasseh. Likewise, since there are, in the Holy Scriptures, many things (112) in a hidden state, this also we will explain at length, namely what sense the words have by which the blessed Jacob made the two sons of Joseph his own (113). The fact of saying, indeed: "the two sons born in Egypt before I came to you in Egypt, are mine; Ephraim and Manasseh, like Reuben and Simeon, shall be mine; as for the younger ones whom you will beget (114) after them, they shall be yours" marks well that (that Jacob makes Ephraim and Manasseh his own). Since indeed Jacob had twelve sons, to whom precisely the twelve tribes owe their

existence, he divided the two sons of Joseph into two tribes; and the tribes were from then on to the number of thirteen, the tribe of Joseph having been divided between his two sons. And thus was he not without having something to do (here), Paul, the Apostle (115). Chosen (116) from among the number of the tribes (Benjamin), he was counted thirteenth after the Apostles, and thus he was, to the Gentiles, as an Apostle, sent.

For the blessing, it (118) concerns someone whom one blesses, while the prophecy is realized at the accomplishment of a certain fact. With these words of the Scripture: "All these are the twelve sons of Jacob; <and> (119) it is thus that their father spoke to them and that he blessed them; he blessed them each according to his blessing (120)," how will the (explanation) proposed above (121) concord: sometimes prophecies manifestly such, sometimes (prophecies) named blessings? (122) In this (explanation) one must understand that it is in the very (things) expressed that lie both blessings and prophecies, in such a way that the blessings, they, fall upon Him who is born of Judah, and upon Him who is prefigured in Joseph, upon Him who, (descended) from Levi, is found to be Priest of the Father, while the prophecies (fall and remain) upon those who acted as adversaries and, for nothing, held the Son of God.

Moreover, that it is indeed so, the Scripture itself will teach us more clearly (123). It says indeed: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you!
ὥωηδοδ δοῶϑϑωα βηϑο ὅ5η6. «οὐὐοδοϑ «οὐ ὀφροηγδο ὅϑος»οῦῦ βρϑογο. φχορὕφοσςο δι με αμμηβα ΚΕ, βρῖν βωῖ βθβ πεμπευῃεῖ, ἡὗξη μμήϊηβ ἀξ. βπερεῖν ηρωῖμι μὴ ἤρα. ἡμερεβμν ἐκ υἰξημῖν ἡμμτᾳ βδοᾳ, μίμμιην μὔρωρ κι μων αὐθάδης" ἐξύδρισας ὡς ὕδωρ, μὴ ἐκζέσῃς: ἀνέδης γὰρ ἐπὶ τὴν κοίτην τοῦ πατρός σου, τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέθης ». τί οὖν, ἐροῦμεν προφητείαν τοῦτο ἢ εὐλογίαν; τὸ γὰρ λέγειν « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης» καὶ τὸ « ἀνέδης ἐπὶ τὴν «( κοίτην 5» τοῦ πατρός σου" τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέθης » φαίνεται ἔλεγχος εἶναι μᾶλλον τοῦ γεγονότος πράγματος ἣ μέλλοντος γενέσθαι. ἀλλ᾽ ἐρεῖ τις, τοῦτο εἰρηκέναι ἐπειδὴ ἹῬουδὴμ. μετὰ Βάλλας τῆς παλλακῆς τοῦ πατρὸς αὐτοῦ κοιμηθεὶς ἐμίανεν τὴν κοίτην τοῦ πατρός. εὑρίσκεται οὖν κατὰ τὸν ἐκείνου λόγον ὁ προφήτης παρ « ῳχηχόσι πράγμασι, οὐ μέλλουσιν; ἐνθάδε παρ 5» ακολουθῶν. ἔν γε τῷ εἰπεῖν αὐτόν᾽ « συνάχθητε υἱοὶ Ἰακώδ, ἵνα ἀπαγγείλω ὑμῖν τὰ συμδησόμενα ὑμῖν ἐπ᾽ ἐσχάτων τῶν ἡμερῶν », περὶ μελλόντων πραγ- μάτων τινῶν αἰνίττεται εἰπεῖν ὁ προφήτης. πῶς οὖν ἐνθάδε νομίσει τις αὐτὸν περὶ
My strength and the leader of my children! Hard in temper (124) and hard in arrogance! You have overflowed with strength, like water. Do not bubble over (with passion)! For you have mounted the couch of your father; then you have defiled the bed where you mounted." What then? Shall we say that we have here prophecy? or blessing? For these words: "Hard in temper and hard in arrogance" and "you have mounted the couch of your father; then you have defiled the bed where you mounted" appear indeed to be blame for a thing having taken place, rather than (blame) for a thing having to take place (125). Eh! but! someone will say, (Jacob) said this once Reuben, after having slept with Bilhah, the concubine of his father, had defiled the couch of his father. On the account of this (opinionated person), the prophet is found therefore <here> to follow by thought <past things, not future ones>. What is certain (126) is that, when he says: "Gather yourselves together, sons of Jacob, that I may announce to you what will happen to you in the last days," it is indeed of certain future things that the prophet lets it be understood that he is going to speak. How then, here, will someone go to think that he speaks of a thing having taken place? As a prophet, indeed, it is on this account that he is judged a prophet,

namely: when it is on future (things) that he makes his narrations, not on past things. One will not look indeed as very strong (127) or extraordinary that, things seen by us two or three years before, we recount them to others in detail, as if, if we are to be believed, we were prophesying them, while with our eyes we have seen the events. Let us therefore show the prophet <as a prophet> (128). He says indeed: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you! my strength and the leader of my children." And this, it is true, some (129) make it apply to the Savior, as being in reality the word of the Father addressing his own Son. But the things expressed immediately after will offend the ears of the readers, and if someone estimates that these (first) words target the Savior, <the words "hard in temper and hard in arrogance" and the following>, he will have to apply (them also to the Savior) (130). But it is not so at all. For Reuben was the firstborn son of Jacob, just as the people also was firstborn, [called] by the Law to <divine> adoption (131). The actions therefore of Reuben, the firstborn, (Jacob) has brought them close to the (actions) which were to be those of the first people. For what he says: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you! my strength and the leader of my children," Jacob may have said it while addressing Reuben himself.

but "hard in impetuosity and hard in arrogance" (he says it) because he penetrates by (prophetic) vision the spirit of indocility and insubordination (132) of his own race (Jacob) (133). And, indeed, Moses likewise said to them: "As for you, you are a stiff-necked people. Take heed to yourselves! Lest the Lord, being enraged (134), come to be angry and exterminate you." By saying: "You have overflowed with force, like water. Do not seethe with passion," Jacob has abated (the pride of) the race which, according to the flesh, is theirs (135), because they (the people of this race) have outraged the Law and treated with the ultimate outrages the Word announced by it. For: "You went up," he says, "to your father's couch. Then you defiled the bed where you went up." Now "couch" and "bed," in his mouth, is the holy flesh of Christ, upon which the Saints, tasting rest as upon a holy divan, are saved (136); flesh which then these lawless ones, after having seized it, outraged, offering to Him (to Christ) vinegar, and striking his head with a reed, and casting lashes of the whip upon his back, and spitting in his face (137), and flaying his


[p. 55] {#po.27.p55}
ὅταν περὶ μελλόντων διηγῆται, οὐ παρῳχηχκότων πραγμάτων. οὐδὲ γὰρ μέγα ἣ παρά- δοξον νομισθήσεται, εἰ τὰ πρὸ δύο ἢ τριῶν ἐτῶν ὑφ᾽ ἡμῶν ἑωραμένα, ταῦτα ὡς δῆθεν προφητεύοντες ἄλλοις ἐκδιηγούμεθα, ὑπὸ ὀφθαλμῶν ἑωρακότες τὰ γεγονότα. δείξωμεν οὖν τὸν προφήτην « ὡς προφήτην Σ-. λέγει γάρ᾽ « Ῥουδὴμ πρωτότοκός μου σὺ εἶ, ἰσχύς μου καὶ ἀρχὴ τέκνων μου ». καὶ τοῦτο μέν τινες φέρουσιν ἐπὶ τὸν σωτῆρα, ὡς δῆθεν τοῦ πατρὸς προσομιλοῦντος τῷ ἰδίῳ υἱῷ. ἀλλὰ τὰ ἐπιφερόμενα ῥητὰ βλάψει τὰς ἀκοὰς τῶν ἐντυγχανόντων. « καὶ Σ- εἰ νομίσει τις τοῦτο πρὸς τὸν σωτῆρα εἰρῆσθαι, « « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης » "-- καὶ τὰ ἀκόλουθα ἐπισυνάψει᾽ ἀλλ᾽ οὐχ οὕτως ἔχει. ὁ
cheeks with slaps, and driving nails (138) into his hands. All this is what the impious and unbelieving people did in concert with the chief priests and the scribes and all the leaders of the people. That is why the blessed prophet neither passed over the work of these in silence, nor does he wish to have anything in common with their wickedness and their counsel (138); but he withdraws for his part from their actions, which involve such criminal machinations. <For he says thus:> (139) Moreover, that it is indeed so, Scripture itself will teach us more clearly (123). It says in effect: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you!
γὰρ ῬῬουδὴμ πρωτότοκος ἦν τοῦ Ἰακὼδ υἱός, ὡς καὶ λαὸς πρωτότοκος ἦν, διὰ τοῦ νόμου εἰς υἱοθεσίαν « θείαν "- κεκλημένος λαός. τὴν οὖν πρᾶξιν τοῦ ἹΡουδήμ, τοῦ πρωτοτόκου, συνέκρινεν πρὸς τὴν μέλλουσαν ἔσεσθαι τοῦ προτέρου λαοῦ. τὸ γὰρ εἰπεῖν αὐτόν" « ἹΡουδὴμ. πρωτότοχός μου σὺ εἶ, ἰσχύς μου καὶ ἀρχὴ τέκνων μου », δύναται πρὸς αὐτὸν τὸν Ρουδὴμ. ἀνειππα δ ας ἰμηξίτα τι! γ ἔστε. ἥηπμδε ἤρπι μεν να μὐιη νει μειῖν, φεεε εινενμειρίτιαα με πῇ ἡτμδπαῖν ματι θῖν, πη πιά μη νεῖν ε σι. μναῖ Ἰαμμι βὲ, ἤδη με εὐπηνμήιβμί, ἡπι, χμιεμπε-: ὁ Ἰακὼθ εἰρηκέναι. τὸ δὲ « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι, καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης » ἐνορῶν τὸ πνεῦμα τὸ ἀπειθὲς καὶ ἀνυπότακτον τοῦ γένους αὑτοῦ. καὶ γὰρ Μωῦσῆς ὁμοίως πρὸς αὐτοὺς ἔφη" « ὑμεῖς δὲ λαὸς σκληροτράχηλός ἐστε. προσέχετε αὑτοῖς, μήποτε θυμωθεὶς ὀργισθῇ κύριος καὶ ἐξολοθρεύσῃ ὑμᾶς ». ἐν δὲ τῷ εἰπεῖν τὸν Ἰακώδ’ « ἐξύθρισας ὡς ὕδωρ, μὴ ἐκζέσῃς » ἐμείωσεν αὐτῶν τὸ κατὰ σάρκα γένος, διὰ τὸ ἐνυδρίσαι αὐτοὺς τῷ νόμῳ καὶ ἐξυθρίσαι αὐτοὺς τὸν διὰ αὐτοῦ κηρυχθέντα λόγον. « ἀνέθης γὰρ » λέγει « ἐπὶ κοίτην τοῦ πατρός σου, τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέδης». κοίτην δὲ καὶ στρωμνὴν εἴρηκεν τὴν ἁγίαν σάρκα Χριστοῦ, ἐφ᾽ ἣν οἱ ἅγιοι ὡς ἐπὶ κλίνην ἁγίαν ἀναπαυόμενοι σώζονται: ἣν τότε λαθόντες « οἱ "- ἄνομοι ἐνύδρισαν ὄξος τούτῳ προσφέροντες καὶ καλάμῳ κεφαλὴν τύπτοντες καὶ μάστιγας νώτῳ ἐπιδάλλοντες καὶ εἰς πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ ἐμπτύοντες καὶ ῥαπίσμασιν σιαγόνας δέροντες καὶ ἥλους χερσὶν ἐμπηγνύοντες"

Do not seethe with passion," Jacob has abated (the pride of) the race which, according to the flesh, is theirs (135), because they (the people of this race) have outraged the Law and treated with the ultimate outrages the Word announced by it. For: "You went up," he says, "to your father's couch. Then you defiled the bed where you went up." Now "couch" and "bed," in his mouth, is the holy flesh of Christ, upon which the Saints, tasting rest as upon a holy divan, are saved (136); flesh which then these lawless ones, after having seized it, outraged, offering to Him (to Christ) vinegar, and striking his head with a reed, and casting lashes of the whip upon his back, and spitting in his face (137), and flaying his

cheeks with slaps, and driving nails (138) into his hands. All this is what the impious and unbelieving people did in concert with the chief priests and the scribes and all the leaders of the people. That is why the blessed prophet neither passed over the work of these in silence, nor does he wish to have anything in common with their wickedness and their counsel (138); but he withdraws for his part from their actions, which involve such criminal machinations. <For he says thus:> (139)

ὁ Ἰακὼθ εἰρηκέναι. τὸ δὲ « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι, καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης » ἐνορῶν τὸ πνεῦμα τὸ ἀπειθὲς καὶ ἀνυπότακτον τοῦ γένους αὑτοῦ. καὶ γὰρ Μωῦσῆς ὁμοίως πρὸς αὐτοὺς ἔφη" « ὑμεῖς δὲ λαὸς σκληροτράχηλός ἐστε. προσέχετε αὑτοῖς, μήποτε θυμωθεὶς ὀργισθῇ κύριος καὶ ἐξολοθρεύσῃ ὑμᾶς ». ἐν δὲ τῷ εἰπεῖν τὸν Ἰακώδ’ « ἐξύθρισας ὡς ὕδωρ, μὴ ἐκζέσῃς » ἐμείωσεν αὐτῶν τὸ κατὰ σάρκα γένος, διὰ τὸ ἐνυδρίσαι αὐτοὺς τῷ νόμῳ καὶ ἐξυθρίσαι αὐτοὺς τὸν διὰ αὐτοῦ κηρυχθέντα λόγον. « ἀνέθης γὰρ » λέγει « ἐπὶ κοίτην τοῦ πατρός σου, τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέθης». κοίτην δὲ καὶ στρωμνὴν εἴρηκεν τὴν ἁγίαν σάρκα Χριστοῦ, ἐφ᾽ ἣν οἱ ἅγιοι ὡς ἐπὶ κλίνην ἁγίαν ἀναπαυόμενοι σώζονται: ἣν τότε λαθόντες « οἱ "- ἄνομοι ἐνύδρισαν ὄξος τούτῳ προσφέροντες καὶ καλάμῳ κεφαλὴν τύπτοντες καὶ μάστιγας νώτῳ ἐπιδάλλοντες καὶ εἰς πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ ἐμπτύοντες καὶ ῥαπίσμασιν σιαγόνας δέροντες καὶ ἥλους χερσὶν ἐμπηγνύοντες" ταῦτα πάντα ἔδρασεν ὁ ἀσεδὴς καὶ ἄπιστος λαὸς σὺν ἀρχιερεῦσιν καὶ γραμματεῦσιν καὶ ΡΟ ρ. ὕὍωφρα Οωϑοσνο οδο σήχδ. φοβὕφορος ὁφροοῦςο φ φορύςφρο οϑδοῶῤοροδω. ϑύρφοϑοςου Ὀγφροσ ἡῶβρδοδὺ «οὐ δϑϑηδοϑοδοῦο δοορδυϑοῦυ ϑοδοῦοδο ὥ.- ϑου». ϑωδρϑὺ ϑυο)ὺ ϑοϑοῶον δ οϑθοςυ ϑοῦο οόθοφυ. ογφηοῦ δῶ ὅρῳ ογορὕϑαρο ὕσθον. δωϑρὺ δοδϑονδδοδοῦ σήηοῦ ὕροοῦ ὠφθοςρο φῦ ὁρρδόβδοῦ οήορδ ὕω Θωδϑορρο ογήσ οὐδωδ. δοσγοῦϑοφο ϑοδοῦδ βϑοοσγοῶρο Τ' " θώδςηο. δηϑρ0 »ῳδῳηρδοῦοα,. ϑουϑοάφοὺ ὁ ρορονοδοο, Ονϑ5). ϑροδοδοῦ ϑχυγφρο .φου ϑοηθορὕ-800 ὥοδοςν οδο ὃ56 ϑοϑοῶσν ϑοῦ ϑογ)ῶ δοβθζ)5ο οδοςοὐδὸ τὸ ὥωϑος» οδο σγήη)5. ϑοογοῦῦϑοςο οβόθφο ὈοΟρθοςοδὺ ϑοϑοῦο ϑοδοῦοδο. ϑοϑοῦ ϑουδοῦο ΘδρτουΘῦ» ϑοῦ Θωϑραοδο οϑόθο Ὀορθοφοῦ τοὺ οὔγσυ οὐθ5ῦ ῦ8ο- ὁῶβον» ἠΘονῥυδονυ. ΙΕ. « Συμεὼν καὶ Λευεὶ ἀδελφοί, συνετέλεσαν ἀδικίαν ἐξαιρέσεως αὐτῶν. εἰς βουλὴν αὐτῶν μὴ ἔλθοι ἣ ψυχή μου « καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπισυστάσει αὐτῶν μὴ ἐρίσαι τὰ ἥπατά μου" 2» ὅτι ἐν τῷ θυμῷ αὐτῶν ἀπέκτειναν ἀνθρώπους καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπιθυμίᾳ ἐνευροκόπησαν ταῦρον. ἐπικατάρατος ὁ θυμὸς αὐτῶν, ὅτι αὐθάδης, καὶ ἡ μῆνις αὐτῶν, ὅτι ἐσκληρύνθη. διαμεριῶ αὐτοὺς ἐν Ἰακὼδ καὶ διασπερῶ αὐτοὺς ἐν Ἰσραήλ. » Ὀχϑοωῦ οὺ φ»580 σου Ὁ «γδ χςσρω,ϑο ὅλο ϑο)ἤο ες» ὅοογ. σγχδοο)».
"Simeon and Levi are brothers. They have committed an injustice together by killing as they have done (140). Into their counsel let not my soul go! <And into their assembly let not my heart quarrel!> (141). Because in their wrath they killed men (142), and, in their covetousness (143), hamstrung the bull. Cursed be their wrath, because arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened! I will divide them in Jacob, and disperse them in Israel." "Simeon and Levi are brothers. They have committed an injustice together by killing as they have done (140). Into their counsel let not my soul go! <And into their assembly let not my heart quarrel!> (141). Because in their wrath they killed men (142), and, in their covetousness (143), hamstrung the bull. Cursed be their wrath, because arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened! I will divide them in Jacob, and disperse them in Israel."

But it is not so. And, indeed, they themselves, then, for their defense, represented to their father that they had done there only a just work. For, to the words of their father: "You have made me hateful, to the point of making me a wicked man in the eyes of all the inhabitants of the land, and toward the Canaanites (147) and toward the Perizzites. Now, I, I am numerically the weakest; and, once leagued against me, they will cut me to pieces and I will be broken into crumbs, I and my people (148)," they replied with these words: "Then? As if she were a prostitute, will they abuse our sister?" It is because they are worthy of praise rather than blame, by reasoning thus, they who, in the full age of youth, have thus, boiling with anger, taken up the cause for their sister then deflowered by the son of Shechem, that Jacob also, and from his mouth, well after these (events), while graciously making a gift of the city

to the blessed Joseph, said (149): "As a choice portion, in preference to all your brothers, I give you Shechem, which I took from the hand of the Amorites with my sword and my bow." As if she were a prostitute, will they abuse our sister?" Read the Gospel and you will find it written. Of the tribe of Simeon were the Scribes; and of that of Levi, the chief priests. Since therefore it is by the counsel of these and the resolution they took there, that Christ was delivered and by them put to death, knowing this in advance, the prophet says: "Into their counsel let not my soul go!" Now he speaks there of the counsel where they deliberated, seeking from where they could launch an accusation against Jesus, so that after having seized him (155) by ruse, they could kill him. As also Isaiah says (it): "Woe to their soul! because in their counsel they have taken against themselves a bad resolution, saying: "Let us put the just one in bonds, since with us he shows himself intractable."
τῷ Ἰου-ΊΥΡ. « Καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπισυστάσει αὐτῶν μὴ ἐρίσαι τὰ ἥπατά μου »" τότε γὰρ ἐπι- συνήχθησαν ἐπ᾽ αὐτὸν πάντες οἱ ἀρχιερεῖς καὶ οἱ γραμματεῖς καὶ οἱ πρεσδύτεροι τοῦ λαοῦ ἐπὶ τὴν οἰκίαν τοῦ ἡγεμόνος, αἰτούμενοι θάνατον κατ᾽ αὐτοῦ. ΡΟ ρ.
"And into their assembly let not my heart quarrel (156)." Then, in effect, against him, all (157) the chief priests and the scribes and the elders of the people gathered at the governor's house, demanding the death penalty against him.

"And, in their covetousness, hamstrung the bull." Now, by saying "their covetousness," he means "the covetousness (they have had) of the world," they who, for having coveted earthly goods, have lost the heavenly ones, once they had killed the Prince of Life, like a bull. "And, in their covetousness, hamstrung the bull." Now, by saying "their covetousness," he means "the covetousness (they have had) of the world," they who, for having coveted earthly goods, have lost the heavenly ones, once they had killed the Prince of Life, like a bull.

Also: "Cursed be their wrath, because arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened!" By their "fury," he meant the anger (160), in which always persisting, they have hardened their neck, "resisting the Holy Spirit." Since therefore, in advance (162), we have said that it is in the terms themselves (which express them) that, on one hand, lie blessings, on the other hand, prophecies (163), it is reasonable, to well persuade those who have the desire to be instructed, not only to demonstrate by reason, but, by the very force of the terms, to make evident these propositions (164). For (Jacob), while blessing Judah, speaks thus: Since therefore, in advance (162), we have said that it is in the terms themselves (which express them) that, on one hand, lie blessings, on the other hand, prophecies (163), it is reasonable, to well persuade those who have the desire to be instructed, not only to demonstrate by reason, but, by the very force of the terms, to make evident these propositions (164). For (Jacob), while blessing Judah, speaks thus: "Judah, you, may your brothers praise you! Your hands (are) on the back of your enemies. The sons of your father will adore you." Then (165): "Lion's whelp (is) Judah. From a shoot, my son, you have gone up. After having bowed your back, you have lain down (166), like a lion and (167) like a lion's whelp. Who will awaken him? There will not lack a leader issued from Judah, nor a conductor (of the people) gone out from his thigh; until he comes to whom it is reserved (to be); and he, will be (168) the expectation of the nations. He is the one who attaches to the vine his donkey and to the orb (of the vine branch), the foal of his donkey. He will wash his robe in wine, and in the

How is it possible that the things here expressed are of the same nature as the things said above against Reuben? There, in effect, he says: "Hard <in impetuosity, and hard> (170) in arrogance, you have overflowed with force, like water. Do not seethe (with passion). For you went up to your father's couch; then, you defiled the bed where you went up." Then (against Simeon and Levi) what is said: "Cursed be their wrath, because arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened!" These are not, in effect, words of blessing, but indeed a reproach, a prophecy being there, regarding them (Reuben, Simeon, Levi) revealed (171). To say on the contrary: "Judah, you, may your brothers praise you! Your hands (are) on the back of your enemies" and (172): "The sons of your father will adore you," that, yes (173), seems indeed to be a blessing.

"But, someone will say (174), for what reason did it seem good to the prophet to impose upon Judah such a blessing, while for the first ones he did nothing of the sort?"
Μάθε. ἐπειδὴ γὰρ ἐκ τῆς Ἰούδα φυλῆς ἔμελλεν ὁ Δαδὶδ γεννᾶσθαι, ἐκ δὲ τοῦ Δαδὶδ τὸ κατὰ σάρκα Χριστός, προγινώσκων «- κατὰ τὸ πνεῦμα τὰ μέλλοντα, εὐλόγησε τὸν ἐκ τοῦ ᾿Ιούδα «- Δαδὶδ "- καὶ « τὸν ἐκ τοῦ "- Δαδὶδ μέλλοντα κατὰ σάρκα γεννᾶσθαι Χριστόν, ἵνα τὴν εὐλογίαν μὴ μόνον τὴν πνευματικὴν ὑπὸ θεοῦ λάθῃ, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὴν κατὰ σάρκα εὐλογίαν. ὡς καὶ διὰ “Ἱερεμία λέγει’ « πρὸ τοῦ με πλάσαι σε ἐν κοιλίᾳ ἐπίσταμαί σε, καὶ πρὸ τοῦ σε ἐξελθεῖν ἐκ μήτρας, ἡγίακά σε καὶ προφήτην εἰς ἔθνη τέθεικά σε ». τὸ δ᾽ ἐκ κοιλίας ἡγιασμένον ἤδη παρὰ θεοῦ εὐλογεῖται. ΡΟ ρ. οὐθοβοὺ Ουϑο0). οσγφοδοῦ δον 8οῦ δὺδ δοδϑοςογδο)ς» οἠῶ φοϑϑοογο ϑοχδδτο
Learn the reason. Since David was to be born of the tribe of Judah, and Christ, as far as the flesh is concerned (175), from David, (the prophet) knowing in advance <future events in their spiritual sense, blessed there David> descending from Judah and Christ who, according to the flesh (175), was to be born of David (176), so that he might receive from God not only the blessing according to the spirit, but also the blessing according to the flesh. As also, through (the mouth of) Jeremiah (God) says: "Before I formed you in the womb of (your mother), I knew you. And before you came out of her breast, I sanctified you and established you as a prophet for the Gentiles." Now, what has been sanctified from the womb (of the mother) is already blessed by God (177).

But, you will say, why then did he not likewise bless Levi? For we find that it is from the tribe of Levi <also> that Christ is shown to us as Priest of the Father, <on the occasion of the mixture that occurred from the tribe of Levi to the tribe of Judah> (178), so that the very fact of being descended from both tribes at once would demonstrate that <the Son> (179) of God is both King and Priest. But, you will say, why then did he not likewise bless Levi? For we find that it is from the tribe of Levi <also> that Christ is shown to us as Priest of the Father, <on the occasion of the mixture that occurred from the tribe of Levi to the tribe of Judah> (178), so that the very fact of being descended from both tribes at once would demonstrate that <the Son> (179) of God is both King and Priest.

Jacob, therefore, on the one hand, penetrating by (prophetic) vision the assault that the princes of the priests, Annas and Caiaphas, dared against the Son of God, for this
Θωϑοφηονο οδοοῶφ᾽ ϑούδδοςυ θοῦ ϑοὺ οοϑογοῦο. «οὐ Ονϑ)). οῃ δῦ οδοῦο ες δε θιωίμημ φιιβνηίμ, ἡπρδαις ρίῶιφιενϊμεινμμνα τίη ρα ἰλήιαι, κα Ἡμνμμεωίναν,, πμ ΡΟ ρ. ἐτόλμησαν ἐπὶ τὸν υἱὸν τοῦ θεοῦ, διὰ τὸ εἶναι αὐτοὺς ἐκ φυλῆς Λευὶ οὐκ ηὐλόγησεν τὸν Λευΐ, ἀλλὰ μᾶλλον αὐτὸν ἐπέπληξεν. ἐλθὼν δὲ ὁ μακάριος Μωῦσῆς τὸν « Χριστὸν τὸν ἐξ ᾿Ααρὼν καὶ Λευὶ γενόμενον ηὐλόγησεν εἰπών’ « δότε Λευΐ δήλους αὐτοῦ, καὶ τὴν ἀλήθειαν αὐτοῦ τῷ ἀνδρὶ τῷ ὁσίῳ ». τὸ οὖν ἐλλεῖπον ἐν ταῖς εὐλογίαις αὐτοῦ Μωυσῆς παρὼν ἀνεπλήρωσεν. καὶ περὶ μὲν τούτων ἐν τῇ διατάξει τῶν Μωυσέως εὐλογιῶν ἀποδώσωμεν τὸν λόγον, νυνὶ δὲ πρὸς τὸ προκείμενον ἐροῦμεν.
that they were of the tribe of Levi, did not bless Levi, but rather strongly rebuked him (180). On the other hand, the blessed (181) Moses, once he had come, blessed <the Christ> (182) descended from Aaron and Levi by saying: "Give to Levi his revelations (183), and his truth to the holy man." What was therefore missing in the Blessings of Jacob, Moses, at his coming, completed. And of this, of course, in the testament (184) that are the Blessings of Moses, we shall render an account. But, for the moment, we shall say what pertains to our subject. Jacob, therefore, speaks thus (185): "Judah, you, may your brothers praise you! Your hands (are) on the back of your enemies. The sons of your father will adore you." Who are the "brothers", those who praised and adored him, if not the Apostles, to whom the Lord said: "You are my brothers and co-heirs"? ΡΟ ρ. μωδ μωιη δωρ ἥδε βημ πῃ Ἰισμία θηϊμωδίνωῃ Κη, ἐαΐς μι «τ ἰες ἐπιμΐνπιιπί κα. ἡμιιπίωι ες μειδιεϊηνη πεῖν μο ζμιε μὲς ἱμωῃίναι « «Σκύμνος λέοντος ᾿Ιούδα ἐχ βλαστοῦ, υἱέ μου, ἀνέθης ». λέοντα οὖν καὶ σκύμνον λέοντος εἰπὼν σαφῶς τὰ δύο πρόσωπα ἐπέδειξεν, τοῦ πατρὸς καὶ υἱοῦ. τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν" «ἐκ βλαστοῦ, υἱέ μου, ἀνέδης », ἵνα τὴν κατὰ σάρκα γέννησιν τοῦ Χριστοῦ δείξῃ, ὃς ἐν κοιλίᾳ παρθένου ἐκ πνεύματος ἁγίου σαρκωθεὶς ἐθλάστησεν ἐν αὐτῇ καὶ ὡς ἄνθος καὶ ὀσμὴ εὐωδίας εἰς κόσμον προελθὼν ἐφανερώθη. ἐπεὶ οὖν σκύμνον λέοντος εἶπεν, τὴν κατὰ πνεῦμα αὐτοῦ γέννησιν ἐκ θεοῦ, ὡς ἐκ βασιλέως βασιλέα γενόμενον ἔδειξεν. οὐκ ἐσιώπησεν δὲ αὐτοῦ καὶ τὴν κατὰ σάρκα, ἀλλά φησιν᾽ «ἐκ βλαστοῦ, υἱέ μου, ἀνέδης». Ησαΐας γὰρ λέγει" « ἐξελεύ- σεται ῥάδδος ἐκ τῆς ῥίζης 144᾽ Ἰεσσαὶ « καὶ ἄνθος ἐξ αὐτῆς 5" ἀναδήσεται ». ἣ οὖν ῥίζα τοῦ ᾿Ιεσσαὶ ἦν τῶν πατέρων γενεὰ ὡς ῥίζα ἐν γῇ πεφυτευμένη, ἡ δὲ ῥάδδος ἣ ἐξ αὐτῶν φανερωθεῖσα ἦν ἡ Μαρία, διὰ τὸ εἶναι ἐξ οἴκου καὶ πατριᾶς Δαδίδ. τὸ δὲ ἄνθος φρὉ85 φορωϑοῦ οὔγφοῦ. ὁρρϑωροδοδοῦδο ὅβϑοφραᾳ βηϑω. δοϑωβόθςο φρῶϑ τοὺ φρῃ δ φραχϑοῦ ογήογϑο)ς». δοδρὈνςογδεγφοος αὐοοῶϑομβο ὈοΌθ. δχβο)οδο ϑοϑοῦσο τ ϑοῦδα οὐ Θωϑὴς» οδο σγής)5. δοσγοϑηςο ὁργϑωροδοδοῦο δὺ6 δοϑωβόοφο. ϑϑοφραω.
"Lion's whelp (is) Judah. From a shoot, my son, you have risen." By saying therefore "lion" and "lion's whelp", he clearly showed the two Persons: that of the Father and that of the Son. He said: "From a shoot, my son, you have risen" to show the generation of Christ according to the flesh, who, incarnate, conceived of the Holy Spirit in the womb of the Virgin, sprouted in her and, like a flower and perfume of sweet odor, once he had come out (of this womb), appeared visibly in the world. Since, therefore, for him, to say (188): "Lion's whelp", was to say his generation according to the spirit (by which) he proceeds from God, he (thereby), as King, born of King, showed him. He did not, however, pass over in silence his generation according to the flesh, but rather said: "From a shoot, my son, you have risen." Isaiah, in fact, says: "A shoot shall come out of the root of Jesse (189) <and a flower from it> (190) shall rise." The root of Jesse was the stock of the Patriarchs, like a root planted in the earth, and the shoot that came out of them, visibly appeared, was Mary, because she was of the House and of the

family of David. The flower, which had sprouted on (191) the shoot, was Christ, which is precisely what Jacob, prophesying, said: "From a shoot, my son, you have risen." "Lion's whelp (is) Judah. From a shoot, my son, you have risen." By saying therefore "lion" and "lion's whelp", he clearly showed the two Persons: that of the Father and that of the Son. He said: "From a shoot, my son, you have risen" to show the generation of Christ according to the flesh, who, incarnate, conceived of the Holy Spirit in the womb of the Virgin, sprouted in her and, like a flower and perfume of sweet odor, once he had come out (of this womb), appeared visibly in the world. Since, therefore, for him, to say (188): "Lion's whelp", was to say his generation according to the spirit (by which) he proceeds from God, he (thereby), as King, born of King, showed him. He did not, however, pass over in silence his generation according to the flesh, but rather said: "From a shoot, my son, you have risen." Isaiah, in fact, says: "A shoot shall come out of the root of Jesse (189) <and a flower from it> (190) shall rise." The root of Jesse was the stock of the Patriarchs, like a root planted in the earth, and the shoot that came out of them, visibly appeared, was Mary, because she was of the House and of the

family of David. The flower, which had sprouted on (191) the shoot, was Christ, which is precisely what Jacob, prophesying, said: "From a shoot, my son, you have risen."

Ἰνδιω ι Ἰνδωι ὁ ᾿
As for: "After having bowed his back, he lay down, like a lion and like a lion's whelp," he said it to clearly show him lying down during the three days of his burial (192), where he remained resting in the heart of the earth. As also the Lord himself testified when he said: "As Jonah spent three days and three nights in the heart of the whale, <so shall the Son of man be in the heart of the earth, for three days and three nights> (193)." And David, announcing it in advance, says: "For my part, I lay down, and fell asleep. I awoke, because the Lord will come to my rescue." Likewise, Jacob also: "Who will awaken him? <and he did not say: "No one awakens him"> (194) but: "Who?" so that we might understand that it is the Father who awakened the Son from the dead; (195) [as] (196) also the Apostle says: "... and from God the Father who awakened him from the dead." And Peter says: "The one whom God awakened, after (delivering him from the pains of death (197), because it was not possible that he should remain under its power (198)."
ΧΥΠΙ. Εἴτα λεπτομερῶς διδάσκων ἡμᾶς τὴν κατὰ σάρκα γέννησιν αὐτοῦ φησίν: «οὐκ ἐκλείψει ἄρχων ἐξ Ἰούδα, οὐδὲ ἡγούμενος ἐκ τῶν μηρῶν αὐτοῦ, ἕως ἂν ἔλθῃ, ᾧ ἀπόκειται, καὶ αὐτὸς ἔσται προσδοκία ἐθνῶν». ὅτι μὴν οὐ κ ἐξέλιπεν ἄρχων οὐδὲ ἡγούμενος ἐκ τῆς φυλῆς ᾿Ιούδα, ἕως παρῆν ὁ σωτήρ, συνέστηκεν" καὶ γὰρ τὸ εὐαγγέλιον ὁμοίως μαρτυρεῖ. καὶ ὅτι γεννηθεὶς ὁ σωτὴρ ἐγένετο προσδοκία ἐθνῶν τῶν ἕως σήμερον εἰς αὐτὸν πιστευόντων, καὶ τοῦτο φανερόν. τίνα γὰρ προσδοκῶμεν ἀπ᾽ οὐρανῶν ἐπὶ τῇ ἡμῶν αὐτῶν σωτηρίᾳ, εἰ μὴ ᾧ ἀπέκειτο πληρῶσαι νόμον καὶ προφήτας; ΡΟ ρ. φῦ ϑορῶϑη ϑοθϑϑοΘοῤοῳ δυβωσδοῦῦ ϑοδοϑδο ὁαῶρου ϑωδοῦν ϑοὺ ϑοθῦ».
Then, instructing us in detail about his generation according to the flesh, he says: "There shall not fail a ruler from Judah, nor a leader (of the people) from his thigh, until he comes to whom it is reserved (to be), and he shall be the expectation of the Gentiles." It is well established, certainly, that there has not failed a ruler, nor a leader (of the people) from the tribe of Judah, until the Savior had come (199). And, in fact, the Gospel likewise attests to it. And that once born the Savior was the expectation of the Gentiles, who until today believe in Him, that also is evident (200). Who, in fact, can we expect (to come) from Heaven for our own salvation, if not Him to whom it was reserved to fulfill the Law and the Prophets?

Then he says: "Behold him tying his donkey to the vine, and the foal of his donkey to the orb (of the vine branch)," showing by this the two calls attached to Him as Vine, and brought together to unity by his love; donkey and foal purified at the same time by a (simple) word (201), upon which the Savior once mounted made his entry into Jerusalem.

"And in the blood of a ripe grape <his> garment." <By garment> of the Word (205), he designates the Gentiles, whoever they were, who were considered as his (206) garment, as, through the prophet, he (the Word) says: "<As surely as I live, says> the Lord, I will clothe them all as a mantle (207)." Since, therefore, he himself, hanging on the wood, was a ripe grape and cluster, he who, pierced in the side, made blood and water gush forth, the latter for a bath (Baptism), the former for a purchase (Redemption), one can no longer <justly> say the prophet (Jacob) said: "He will wash his robe in wine, and, in the blood of a ripe grape, his garment (208)."
