more than milk." "Eyes" therefore, eyes of Christ, were the prophets, rejoicing in the strength of the Spirit, and announcing in advance the sufferings that were to fall upon him, which, even to the generations that came after him, served, so that having believed, <every> (209) man could be saved. As for what he says: "White (are) his teeth more than milk," he (thereby) signified, either the Apostles sanctified by the Word <himself> and become like milk (210), they who communicated to us the spiritual and heavenly food; or, undoubtedly, also, saying this, he means the commandments (211) of the Lord, which came out of a holy mouth, but for us are and remain milk, so that, holding from them nourishment and growth, we might, from the heavenly bread also, take our share. Then, showing (thereby) clearly, at the same time, his prophets and his apostles, he says: "With joy, the wine makes his eyes shine, and white are his teeth"

trials, seeking to anchor and to take refuge in ports: the Churches. For by "anchorage of ships" <which make a stop at the cities> (214) he well said the Churches which are in the world like widely spread ports, become a refuge for the believers. As also the prophet says: "Land of Zebulun and land of Naphtali, by the ways of the sea, beyond the Jordan! The people sitting in <darkness have seen a great light; those who were sitting in> the shadow of death, a light has risen upon them... [the faith] of the people (215)." Then he says: "Zebulun will inhabit the seashore, and (he is), himself, at the anchorage of ships; and he will stretch as far as Sidon (212)." trials, seeking to anchor and to take refuge in ports: the Churches. For by "anchorage of ships" <which make a stop at the cities> (214) he well said the Churches which are in the world like widely spread ports, become a refuge for the believers. As also the prophet says: "Land of Zebulun and land of Naphtali, by the ways of the sea, beyond the Jordan! The people sitting in <darkness have seen a great light; those who were sitting in> the shadow of death, a light has risen upon them... [the faith] of the people (215).

On the other hand, by saying: "He will stretch as far as Sidon," he showed the long-suffering of God who stretched the epochs until the last days, so that, they too, the Gentiles, who now believe, could be saved. Sidon, in fact, is the first-born son of Ham who, because of his sin, fell under the curse of his father (216); (Ham) whose lineage precisely, for its faith (217) in Christ, is blessed.

Without a doubt, metaphorically, by allegory, he compares Issachar to the [S]avior (218), who alone, from the age when he did not yet speak, desired the Beautiful, (as Isaiah also well says: "Before the little child knows how to call father and mother, he says "no" to Evil to choose the Good, resting, [in] (219) the heritage of the prophets, to accomplish that of which they had only spoken. And, indeed, on the mountain, Moses and Elijah were seen, on the right and on the left, conversing with Him so that (220) the Savior might be shown taking his rest in their midst. Without a doubt, metaphorically, by allegory, he compares Issachar to the [S]avior (218), who alone, from the age when he did not yet speak, desired the Beautiful, (as Isaiah also well says: "Before the little child knows how to call father and mother, he says "no" to Evil to choose the Good, resting, [in] (219) the heritage of the prophets, to accomplish that of which they had only spoken. And, indeed, on the mountain, Moses and Elijah were seen, on the right and on the left, conversing with Him so that (220) the Savior might be shown taking his rest in their midst.

"Dan", he says, "will judge his people, as if there were only one tribe in Israel. This which precisely - and this in part - took place for Samson, who, issuing by his birth from the tribe of Dan, judged the people for twenty years but (which) totally will be accomplished for the Antichrist, who, born a terrible judge and tyrant King, on one hand will judge his own people, on the other hand will offer opposition - "serpent coiled on the edge of a path - [the] word” (226, note 4) of the Truth; striving to bite (226, note 5) those who walk straightly. (means): tempting those who by their preaching show the path of truth and salvation. As he (the serpent) tempted even the apostles, and, from Judas, after having deceived him, <seized> and <like a horse, made him fall and> (226, note 9) delivered the rider [who] [was riding him] (226, note 9) to death. As "a rider backward", that is to say by going back from the end to the origin, having awaited salvation from the Lord, he (the Savior) made Adam (that is to say humanity) rise and set him straight on his feet in his falls (227). "Dan", he says, "will judge his people, as if there were only one tribe in Israel. This which precisely - and this in part - took place for Samson, who, issuing by his birth from the tribe of Dan, judged the people for twenty years but (which) totally will be accomplished for the Antichrist, who, born a terrible judge and tyrant King, on one hand will judge his own people, on the other hand will offer opposition - "serpent coiled on the edge of a path - [the] word” (226, note 4) of the Truth; striving to bite (226, note 5) those who walk straightly. (means): tempting those who by their preaching show the path of truth and salvation. "Gad, a Chamber of Trials will put him to the test and He, in his turn, immediately, will put them to the test" (228) "Chamber of Trials", in his mouth, designates the Sanhedrin, malignantly wicked, of the Princes of the Priests and the Scribes, who were continually putting the Savior to the test by various trials, in the desire they had to find a grievance from which to launch an accusation against him, in order, once they had, by ruse, seized him, to kill him. He, [knowing] (229) their calculations, retaliating, [them]

put to the test, (remaining) in his own justice, and (they, remaining) in their personal sin, he delivered (them) (230) to death. As also well in the Gospel it is written: "Then approached him some of the Pharisees, saying: "Master, tell us by what power you do these things, and who is the one who gave you this power?" And he said to them: "I will also ask you. One single question. If you answer me (231), I also will make known to you by what power I do these things. The Baptism of John, from where was it? From Heaven or from men?" (232). Now they were making this reasoning within themselves, saying to each other: "If we say: from Heaven, he will say to us: "Why did you not believe in him? If we say: from men, we have to fear the people (233). For all have this opinion of John that he is truly a prophet". And in response to Jesus they said: "We do not know”. And he, in response, said to them: "And neither do I tell you by what power I do these things. Beautifully therefore the prophet said: "A Chamber of Trials will [put him to the test]", and: "But he immediately [will put them to the test]" (234). "Gad, a Chamber of Trials will put him to the test and He, in his turn, immediately, will put them to the test" (228) "Chamber of Trials", in his mouth, designates the Sanhedrin, malignantly wicked, of the Princes of the Priests and the Scribes, who were continually putting the Savior to the test by various trials, in the desire they had to find a grievance from which to launch an accusation against him, in order, once they had, by ruse, seized him, to kill him. Φυϑοο). 9οδϑῦφ»ο οβῶφοοῦδ οωδοῦρδο δοογοῶϑῃς θοδοονβοῶϑηδειξ)δθρο φοοτοο ὁθὼ ϑοσγδοῦ τὺ Ξ3Θβησῦ. ο5 δῶν θϑοο, Τ' ἃ φῦ ον». σῶμ᾽ Ὀοδϑ5 ὃ δοονδῶυ Φωϑφροον» ὁοφηϑθοργρδοονὺ ὁϑοῖ δ δϑοροφοῦφο θοδοουθοϑοῥοε)οθθο Ψ ὁδὸ οὐε5ῦ. ὀόδροουϑοδ δοδρδφοωῦ οδο. τοὺ ϑοῦβὺ δοδροςοδρῦδ οδοῦο
put to the test, (remaining) in his own justice, and (they, remaining) in their personal sin, he delivered (them) (230) to death. As also well in the Gospel it is written: "Then approached him some of the Pharisees, saying: "Master, tell us by what power you do these things, and who is the one who gave you this power?" And he said to them: "I will also ask you. One single question. If you answer me (231), I also will make known to you by what power I do these things. The Baptism of John, from where was it? From Heaven or from men?" (232). Now they were making this reasoning within themselves, saying to each other: "If we say: from Heaven, he will say to us: "Why did you not believe in him? If we say: from men, we have to fear the people (233). For all have this opinion of John that he is truly a prophet". And in response to Jesus they said: "We do not know”. And he, in response, said to them: "And neither do I tell you by what power I do these things. Beautifully therefore the prophet said: "A Chamber of Trials will [put him to the test]", and: "But he immediately [will put them to the test]" (234).

"Asher, fattening is his bread; and he will distribute (235) food to the chiefs. Here therefore the prophet speaks in veiled words, either of the Apostles, whose office it was to provide and to share (236) the bread of life, or of the Savior; (the prophet) announcing in advance, or making known to us (237), the bread descended from Heaven, which is food and drink for the saints. Asher in fact translates as "richness" (238), seeing that alone being rich he was capable of satiating all (239) those who came to him. And, indeed, Christ (240) in person, attesting it of himself, said: "It is I who am the bread, the (bread) descended from Heaven. [Your] (241) fathers ate manna in the desert, and they died; he who eats of my bread will not see death eternally. Now it is necessary, this also, to seek to understand. For he did not say: "He will not die”, but: "He will not see death eternally", which precisely is the eternal punishment by fire, which is eternal death never having any cease. For the (death) of now is death [which arrives in a single day] (242), and to all men "Asher, fattening is his bread; and he will distribute (235) food to the chiefs. Here therefore the prophet speaks in veiled words, either of the Apostles, whose office it was to provide and to share (236) the bread of life, or of the Savior; (the prophet) announcing in advance, or making known to us (237), the bread descended from Heaven, which is food and drink for the saints. Asher in fact translates as "richness" (238), seeing that alone being rich he was capable of satiating all (239) those who came to him. And, indeed, Christ (240) in person, attesting it of himself, said: "It is I who am the bread, the (bread) descended from Heaven. [Your] (241) fathers ate manna in the desert, and they died; he who eats of my bread will not see death eternally. Now it is necessary, this also, to seek to understand. For he did not say: "He will not die”, but: "He will not see death eternally", which precisely is the eternal punishment by fire, which is eternal death never having any cease. For the (death) of now is death [which arrives in a single day] (242), and to all men

αἰώνιος μηδέποτε παυόμενος" ὁ γὰρ νῦν προσήμερος ἐστὶν θάνατος, καὶ πάντας ἀνθρώπους δεῖ ἀποθανεῖν" ὥστε οὐ περὶ τούτου τοῦ θανάτου ἔλεγεν ὁ κύριος, ἀλλὰ περὶ τοῦ μέλλοντος.
it is necessary to die; so much so that it is not of this death that the Lord was speaking, but of the death to come.

"Naphtali (is) a vine-shoot in its growth delivered (243), giving in addition, in its shoots, beauty. "Vine-shoot in its growth delivered” signifies a people called, with a view to giving it, through faith, freedom; so that all might have power to bear fruit for God (244). For, on one hand, the spiritual Vine was the Savior; the branches and the vine-shoots, on the other hand, are his saints, those who believe in Him; and the clusters, his martyrs; and the trees that one marries to the vine, show the Passion (245); and the grape-gatherers (are) the Angels; and the baskets, in which are gathered the fruits of the vine, are the Apostles; and the winepress, the Church; and the wine, the strength of the Holy Spirit. To say therefore of the "vine-shoot" that it is "in its growth delivered" (is to say it) freed from the bonds of death. As [Malachi] (246) also well says: "You will go out and leap (247), like

calves delivered from (their) bonds. As for: "Giving in addition (248), in its shoots, beauty", it is because, in the regeneration [by] water (249), they acquire the grace and the beauty of the Word who, "in his flower, surpassed in beauty the sons of men. "Naphtali (is) a vine-shoot in its growth delivered (243), giving in addition, in its shoots, beauty. "Vine-shoot in its growth delivered” signifies a people called, with a view to giving it, through faith, freedom; so that all might have power to bear fruit for God (244). For, on one hand, the spiritual Vine was the Savior; the branches and the vine-shoots, on the other hand, are his saints, those who believe in Him; and the clusters, his martyrs; and the trees that one marries to the vine, show the Passion (245); and the grape-gatherers (are) the Angels; and the baskets, in which are gathered the fruits of the vine, are the Apostles; and the winepress, the Church; and the wine, the strength of the Holy Spirit. To say therefore of the "vine-shoot" that it is "in its growth delivered" (is to say it) freed from the bonds of death. As [Malachi] (246) also well says: "You will go out and leap (247), like

"Joseph! my son become great! <my blessed son> (250) whom one envies! my son (of my sons) the youngest! Return to me! Against him, plotting, insulted him (251) and set themselves against him, commanders of troops of archers. And with sovereign force (252) were broken their bows, and paralyzed the muscles of their arms, of their [hand[s]] (253), by the hand of the Mighty One of Jacob. It is from there that comes He who strengthens Israel: from the God of your father (254). And has come to your aid the God who is my God, and he has given you his blessing: blessing of Heaven, on high, and blessing of the earth which contains everything. was for him a son of old age. To the world, indeed, already aging and entirely lost, the Son of God, having manifested himself, and having been born of the Virgin, appeared to the eyes of the Father as a 'Son of old age,' he (the Son) who, from before the ages, always, is with God. It is for this that (the Father) says to him: 'Return to me!', recalling his own child from the earth to the heavens. 'Son whom I have blessed (262), who is envied! For, while he had been blessed before God (263), at his advent, in his own home, he was not recognized (264), but envied and hated. And, indeed, the Scripture says: 'Now his brothers having seen that the father loved him, were jealous of him, and, even more, continued to hate him (265)'. ΡΟ ρ. τῷ κόσμῳ καὶ παντελῶς ἀπολωλότι ἐπιφανοῖς ὃ υἱὸς τοῦ θεοῦ καὶ ἐκ παρθένου γεννηθεὶς υἱὸς γήρους ἀπεδείχθη τῷ πατρὶ πρὸ τῶν αἰώνων ἀεὶ συνὼν τῷ θεῷ. διὰ τοῦτο πρὸς αὐτὸν λέγει « πρός με ἀνάστρεψον », ἐκ γῆς εἰς οὐρανοὺς προσκαλούμενος τὸν αὑτοῦ παῖδα. was for him a son of old age. To the world, indeed, already aging and entirely lost, the Son of God, having manifested himself, and having been born of the Virgin, appeared to the eyes of the Father as a 'Son of old age,' he (the Son) who, from before the ages, always, is with God. It is for this that (the Father) says to him: 'Return to me!', recalling his own child from the earth to the heavens.

"From there comes He who strengthens Israel: from the God of <your> (277) father. And the God who is my God has come to your aid. Who then is he who has strengthened Israel and has come to the aid of his own child, if not God alone? As also well, through Isaiah, he (God) says: 'Jacob, my child (278), I will support him; Israel, my chosen one, my soul has delighted in him.' And again he says: 'Do not fear, because you have been confounded (279); nor be ashamed, because you have been outraged.' This being so, in compensation for the violence <of which he was the object>, giving him in return the blessings (280), he says: 'From there comes He who strengthens Israel: from the God of <your> (277) father. And the God who is my God has come to your aid. Who then is he who has strengthened Israel and has come to the aid of his own child, if not God alone? As also well, through Isaiah, he (God) says: 'Jacob, my child (278), I will support him; Israel, my chosen one, my soul has delighted in him.' And again he says: 'Do not fear, because you have been confounded (279); nor be ashamed, because you have been outraged.' This being so, in compensation for the violence <of which he was the object>, giving him in return the blessings (280), he says:'

"By virtue of the blessing of the breasts, and of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother (285)". "Of the blessing of the breasts". That is, of the two Testaments, from which came the preaching announcing in advance the future appearance of the Word in the world; breasts thanks to which (286) both He has milk for us, and He feeds (us) (287), presenting us to God as children. Or else, these are the breasts of Mary, (breasts) that he suckled, which he designates there, (breasts) which were blessed, to which - and in an acclamation - a woman said: "Blessed is the womb that bore you! and the breasts that you have suckled."

By adding and saying: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother" the prophet proclaims in advance a spiritual mystery. He could, indeed, have said: "And of the blessing of the womb of your mother", in order, by this expression, to indicate Mary, by the womb of whom the Word was carried for nine months. Well! that is not what he said, but rather he says: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother (288)." Of these two things, by having thus joined them together, he has made but one, so that it might be well understood that to this

person belongs <both what is according to the spirit and what is> according to the flesh (289). For the Word drew his origin from a heart of a father and from holy [entrails], born <as he was> (290) from a womb of a father (291). As also well, through the prophet, he (the Father) says: "My heart has uttered a good Word." On the other hand, according to the flesh, in the last times, he drew his origin from a virginal womb after having been carried there for nine months, so that, after being born a second time, from a womb of a mother also, he might appear visible (292). <As also well, through the prophet, (he himself) says: "Thus says the Lord> (293) who formed me from the womb (of my mother) to be his servant." And, through Jeremiah, (the Father) says: "Before forming you in the womb (of your mother), I knew you; and before you had come out of her womb, I sanctified you." Since then the Word was begotten both according to the spirit and according to the flesh, as being in fact both God and man, it is with reason that the prophet (Jacob) has employed the name of "womb" for a father and for a mother; which (294), if it were not thus understood, could appear to some

laughable. For the term "womb" can only well follow after the female nature alone. Now here he said: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother" so that precisely you might understand that the Word is begotten of two substances, of God and of the Virgin. That is why he adds still saying: By adding and saying: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother" the prophet proclaims in advance a spiritual mystery. He could, indeed, have said: "And of the blessing of the womb of your mother", in order, by this expression, to indicate Mary, by the womb of whom the Word was carried for nine months. Well! that is not what he said, but rather he says: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother (288)." laughable. For the term "womb" can only well follow after the female nature alone. Now here he said: "And of the blessing of the womb of your father and of your mother" so that precisely you might understand that the Word is begotten of two substances, of God and of the Virgin. That is why he adds still saying:

"You have surpassed the blessings of the mountains that endure (295), and the desires (296) of the eternal hills." For over all principalities (297), prophets and apostles, he (the Word) has surpassed, and has been glorified, exalted as he is by God inheriting; upon his head the blessing in its entirety. Of all the Saints, indeed, Christ was head.

"They will be, indeed, (these blessings), says (Jacob), upon the head of Joseph and upon the top of the skull of the brothers whom he commanded." "Of his brothers", he said: not his brothers according to the flesh, for those he rejected, but those who were esteemed (298) to be his brothers according to the spirit, those to whom the Lord said: "My brothers and co-heirs." It is upon those that Jacob said the blessings would come, those whom the Savior himself will be able to regard as his own brothers (299).

"Benjamin! ravenous wolf! In the morning, he will still devour; and in the evening, he gives (299 bis) the food." "Ravenous wolf": it is therefore very obviously the Apostle Paul that he has, by this name, designated; (Paul) who was born of the tribe of Benjamin; ravenous wolf at the beginning (300), tearing and devouring the sheep of the Church. As Paul himself also confesses, saying: "I have nothing of what is needed to be called an apostle, because, to excess, I persecuted the Church of God. But it is by the grace of God that I am what I am."

It is moreover for this that Rachel, she too, giving birth to Benjamin, called him then by this name "Son of my pain," prophesying what was to happen, namely that Paul, born of the tribe of Benjamin, was to cause pain (301) and tribulation to his own mother, that is to say to the Church, by causing to perish all those who called upon the name of the Lord. (Paul) who, after having been, at the beginning, a blasphemer and persecutor, later, once converted, distributed to all the spiritual and heavenly food, being himself the first to announce Christ to the Gentiles (302); to whom keeping our faith (303), let us also, give praise to God (304), for to Him is the glory in the ages of ages. Amen.
